# Code map changelog

Session-by-session history of code-map updates — **one dated bullet per session note,
newest first**. This file replaces the old habit of prepending each session's note into
`_meta.json` `"notes"` (which had grown to ~90K chars and made the file unreadable).

**Convention for new sessions:** add your note as a new bullet at the **top** of the list
below, and set `_meta.json` `"notes"` to that same note plus the pointer to this file —
never accumulate history in `"notes"`.

- **2026-09-07** — (branch `docs/656-f16-bookkeeping`, Option-B bookkeeping follow-up for PR #656, mapped at `e02851af`) — **F16 (list caps, pagination and date windows) mapped surgically.** `api.md`: `invoices/` gains a dense F16 bullet — `KPI_SUMMARY_EXCLUDED`/`LIFETIME_INVOICED_EXCLUDED` (`invoice-status-sets.ts`), `getKpiSummary(today,user)` (`GET /invoices/kpi-summary`, declared before `:id`, OPERATOR+CUSTOMER-scoped) replacing the invoices page's `useInvoices({limit:999})` reduce (B12) — one uncapped `findMany` bucketed in Node (perf follow-up filed, below), plus `invoicePayment.count`+one `$queryRaw` for `avgDays`; B89's per-column day-window fix (UTC-midnight `lte`, tenant-local `paidAt` via new `tenantCalendarDayStart`/`tenantCalendarDayEnd`, `duplicate()`'s `issueDate`); B169's array-form `orderBy` id tiebreakers + the `Object.hasOwn` allowlist guard (m9) at all 4 sort sites. `orders/` gains B144 (search composes with role/customerId scope instead of an `else if`, `@Max(MAX_LIST_LIMIT)`) + B169. `customers/` gains B110 (uncapped DB-side statement figures + `lifetimeInvoiced`/`lifetimeReceived` aggregates + `transactionsTruncated`, `roundMoney`d; the `take:100/50/50` reads stay ledger-DISPLAY-only; the per-row ledger `amount`/`runningBalance` are still unrounded — filed, not fixed) + B169. `supplier-statements/` gains the new shared `matchable-bills.ts` (`fetchMatchableBills`, B117 — no `take` at all, `orderBy` id-tiebreakered, guarded against re-duplication by `matchable-bills.guard.spec.ts`). `web.md`: new bullets on `orders/page.tsx` (B144, server-side search, pager always renders), `finance/payments/[id]/page.tsx` (B80, `usePaymentDetail`), `invoices/page.tsx` (B12, `useInvoiceKpiSummary`, spec 22's `summaryBarQuery` helper re-pointed), `customers/[id]/page.tsx` (B110, `meta.total` order count, `LedgerTruncationNote`); a new `e2e/37-list-caps.spec.ts` e2e-table row (project `list-caps`) documenting the post-deploy run in full, including the harness finding below. `mobile.md` gains a 2026-09-07 section: `(customer)/payments.tsx` and `(operator)/customers/[id]/statement.tsx` truncation notes, `lib/api/customers.ts` `transactionsTruncated`. `packages.md` gains `api/invoices.ts` (NEW file, `InvoiceKpiSummary`) and the `api/buyer.ts` `BuyerStatement` additions. **Registry:** proved B89/B117/B169 (T1, exact `it()`-title evidence) and B12 (T2 — both its unit-test evidence AND its e2e leg, which PASSED post-deploy) against PR #656; `discharge F16` landed all four as `done`. **B80/B110/B144 (all T2) were NOT proven or discharged** — the deployment-triggered E2E (GitHub Actions run 34137751080, headSha `e02851af`, project `list-caps`) ran 140 passed/4 failed/25 skipped: REG-B12 passed; REG-B80/REG-B144/REG-B110 each failed on all 3 attempts with the IDENTICAL error `"operator storageState carried no access token"`; the fourth failure, `REG-B11` in `22-payment-truth.spec.ts`, is a SEPARATE, REAL, LIVE regression (not a flake — the "Awaiting confirmation" KPI tile reads 0 after a DRAFT payment; team-board diagnosis: `getKpiSummary`'s `awaitingConfirmationCount` scoping and/or a missing cache invalidation) with its own hotfix light loop already launched elsewhere (`fix-round-5-hotfix.md`) — not touched in this session, see `web.md`'s spec-37 entry for the full note. Root-caused (not merely suspected): all three tests call `operatorAccessToken(page)` — which reads `localStorage` on the CURRENT page — as their first statement, before any `page.goto(...)`, so it evaluates on an unnavigated page; Playwright only restores a `storageState`'s `localStorage` once the browser has actually navigated to the matching origin. REG-B12 (which navigates first and never calls the helper) passed in the same file/run, and precedent specs `21-destructive-guards.spec.ts`/`22-payment-truth.spec.ts` both always call `page.goto` before reading the token — `37-list-caps.spec.ts` (new in #656) broke that established ordering. This reads as a TEST-HARNESS defect in the new spec, not a product regression in B80/B110/B144's server+web fixes (each has its own passing T1 unit/RTL coverage). Left `queued`; a `note` on each row records the finding + run id, and a `spawn_task` suggestion (fix the `operatorAccessToken` ordering, re-run, then prove/discharge) was flagged for a follow-up session — never re-run or resumed inside this bookkeeping session. B100 `note`d as SPLIT to its own run (F16b, per `cause-ruling.md` §9 — owner ack required before its migration), left `queued`. Filed 10 new unbatched rows from the sibling sweep `fix-round-4.md` already scoped: **B235** (`getKpiSummary`'s Node-side bucketing, perf), **B236/B237/B238** (estimates/vendor-bills/credit-notes pages' own `limit:999` KPI fetch-alls, same class as B12), **B239/B240** (`import.service.ts`'s host-local `setHours` dedup window + `take:100` orphan count), **B241** (`duplicate-match.service.ts`'s two `take:200` candidate-pool matches), **B242** (buyer finances' Active-Credits note over-firing on the combined `transactionsTruncated` flag), **B243** (customer statement ledger rows' unrounded float `amount`/`runningBalance`), **B244** (`ci-freshness-guard-script.spec.ts` flaky under concurrent host load). `sync`/`sync --check` and `campaign-check.mjs` both green (244 records mirror the ledger; api/web/mobile/pricing jest reports regenerated fresh first — 4 pre-existing api suite failures, all environment-only: `@routeflow/pricing`'s `dist` not built + a missing `squawk` binary on this host, unrelated to this docs-only change; `ci-freshness-guard-script.spec.ts` was NOT among them, so no isolated re-run was needed). Lesson **L-089** appended (domain — a `take`/`limit` chosen as a rendering budget was reused as an arithmetic boundary across six sites; a total/lookup/match/search is computed by the DB over the whole (open) set or labelled partial, and every paginated order carries an id tiebreaker); **L-080** (process, guarded by `scripts/campaign/bugs.mjs` self-test's `sync --check` T13/T13b + `move --tier` T14, which `npm run verify` runs before every push) archived for byte headroom — the next-oldest active entry after L-079 with a landed automated-artifact guard uncited outside `.claude/pipeline`/`.claude/lessons`/this file (grep-confirmed; #653's note already established no entry between L-079 and L-080 qualifies). - **2026-09-06** — (branch `docs/643-f18-bookkeeping`, Option-B bookkeeping follow-up for PR #643, mapped at `7a2b112b`) — **F18 (plan-change routing, clawback lock, add-on refusal) mapped surgically.** `api.md` billing section: new bullets for `addon.service.ts` `enableAddon`/`disableAddon` entitlement-follows-Stripe refusals (B107), `subscription-mutation.service.ts` `planChangePreview()` classification/one-armed-transition invariant (B58), `billing-cron.service.ts` `applyScheduledDowngrades()`'s ACTIVE/non-deleted tenant filter; the `updatePlan`/`activateManualSubscription` bullet gained a sentence on clearing the same downgrade markers; the `commission-engine.service.ts` bullet gained the unconditional `tenantTransaction`-wrapped accrual lock (B73). `web.md`: new "Plan-change routing" bullet (`lib/api/billing.ts` `dispatchPlanChange`, `choose-plan/page.tsx` routing + seat-ack gate, `settings/billing/page.tsx`'s "Keep current plan" button) and a `33-change-plan-routing.spec.ts` row in the e2e table. Two tooling-shaped findings from the same sweep (a `campaign-check`/pre-push `GIT_DIR` freshness-gate false-refusal, and a flaky script-contract spec under host load) were NOT filed into this registry — no tooling shard or backlog is named anywhere in INDEX.md/DECISIONS.md/CLAUDE.md and the 214-row catalogue held zero tooling-classed entries before this session; reported to the lead instead of guessed into the product registry. `sync --check` and `campaign-check.mjs` both green (api + mobile jest reports regenerated fresh first). Lesson **L-084** appended (tooling, campaign-check `GIT_DIR`/`GIT_WORK_TREE` freshness false-refusal under the pre-push hook) — note the task brief that specified this follow-up named it L-083, but master already carries an unrelated L-083 (`campaign-check freshness`, merged earlier the same day) and `_meta.json.nextId` was already `84` before this session touched it, so this entry is filed as the actually-free **L-084**; the two oldest active entries whose Guard names a landed automated artifact were archived for headroom.
- **2026-09-06** — (worktree `rf-registry`, branch `fix/campaign-check-report-freshness`, fix-round 1 over Opus's refute-first review; `.claude/pipeline/2026-09-06-campaign-check-freshness/fix-round-1-ruling.md` + `review-opus.md`) — **campaign-check freshness guard: first-parent bound, partial reasons, clock-skew clamp, merge tests (F1–F5).** F1 (blocker): `newestCommit` now runs `git log` with `--first-parent` — plain `git log -1 -- <path>` is pruned by git's default history simplification whenever a merge is TREESAME to one parent for that path, so a branch that `git merge`s in the ledger shards (or a workspace's tests) from elsewhere without touching them itself was judged by the OLDER upstream commit's time, never the merge's own — the exact population of refusals R1 exists to catch, reproduced with real git in new spec **T15** (a `--no-ff` merge fixture; both `git log -1` with and without `--first-parent` are asserted directly, not just eyeballed). F2: `jest-campaign-reporter.cjs`'s `computePartial` now sets `partial: true` on `testPathPatterns.isSet()` alone (a non-array `.patterns` shape used to fail OPEN to `partial: false`) and adds `onlyChanged`/`changedSince`/`shard`/`findRelatedTests` as independent triggers with synthetic reason labels — **T12** gains a third case (`{ onlyChanged: true }`). F3: each staleness bound now clamps to `Date.now()` before comparing, with one `campaign-check: note — commit <sha> is dated in the future (<ISO>); clock skew? treating it as now` per offending commit — a commit dated ahead of the real clock (a fast dev-box clock, a hand-set `GIT_COMMITTER_DATE`) no longer hard-blocks every future report forever (new **T17**). F5: new **T16** pins pathspec scoping (a later commit touching neither pathspec must never mark a fresh report stale — bounding by HEAD instead would fail this). Spec's `T0` anchor changed from the fixed literal `1_800_000_000` to `Date.now()/1000 - 86400` — the literal was already ~130 days ahead of the real wall clock when this round landed, which F3's new clock-skew clamp would have mistaken for a skewed commit in every one of T1–T14's own fixtures; only relative offsets among the (now 17) cases matter. - **2026-09-06** — (worktree `rf-registry`, branch `fix/bugs-selftest-lock-liveness`, off `master` `0b715128`) — **Lock-liveness self-test pid-reuse fixture flake fixed (CI run 34019219777; `.claude/pipeline/2026-09-06-lock-liveness/reader.md` + `ruling.md`).** The (f) PID-REUSE fixture in `scripts/campaign/bugs.mjs` forged a stale owner's `bootAt` as `Date.now() - LOCK_ABANDON_MS*10` (20 min before "now") instead of relative to the machine's real boot; `BOOT_STAMP_SLOP_MS` only measures the GAP between the forged and real stamps, so a `ubuntu-latest` runner whose actual `os.uptime()` happened to be ~20 minutes when the self-test ran made the impostor's forged boot read as the SAME boot, and the waiter spun its whole `LOCK_SPIN_MS` budget and gave up 1 for 1 — the three checks after it (the write-landed assertion and the (g) OWNER-WRITE FAILURE precondition) inherited the mess purely because the fixtures share `lockDir` state, not from any independent defect. Fix, `bugs.mjs` self-test fixtures only (no lock-primitive change — `acquireLock`/`pidAlive`/`bootStamp`/slop/spin are unchanged and correct): the forged `bootAt` is now `bootStamp() - 365 * 24 * 3600_000` (one year before the REAL boot, never within 5 s of it on any conceivable uptime); the two checks that used to assert exit-code-then-regex separately are folded into ONE verdict+end-state check (`{code, verdict: /predates this boot/.test(out), ageBased: /age|abandon|last resort/i.test(out)}` against `{0, true, false}`) so a regression that makes `breakWhy` fall through to the age-based LAST-RESORT branch instead of the boot-mismatch branch can't hide behind a bare passing exit code; the pid-reuse block now kills AND awaits the impostor child (`awaitExit`, the file's own pre-existing dead-holder-fixture idiom) and clears its own `lockDir` in a `finally`, so a failure inside it can never leak a process or wedge the next fixture; the (g) owner-write-failure fixture now clears `lockDir` itself immediately before its own "no lock exists" precondition check instead of assuming the (f) fixture left a clean slate — that precondition now proves this fixture's own setup, never the previous one's cleanliness; and a give-up print (`r.out.slice(-1200)`) now precedes the write-landed check so a future regression's `got` line is legible instead of a bare `"T2"`. Self-test check count: 290 → 289 in this file (the one ruling-approved fold; every other check preserved 1:1, still `self-test: all checks passed`). Lesson **L-082** (testing) appended; the oldest active LESSONS.md entry whose Guard names an automated artifact was archived to clear the byte cap (see `ARCHIVE.md`, `_meta.json`). Gates: `bugs.mjs self-test`, `prettier --check scripts/campaign/*.mjs`, `bugs.mjs sync --check`, `node scripts/validate-lessons.mjs`. No new files, no schema/ledger/lock-semantics change.

- **2026-09-05** — (worktree `rf-imp-E`, branch `feat/legacy-orphan-users-and-ocr-review`, off `origin/master` `17e81c2c` = #626; code commit `9f367266`) — **THE BACKFILL TOOL LEARNS UNATTENDED WRITES, BOUNDED TO APPROVED TEST TENANTS (`--only-test-tenants` + a restricted `--confirm`).** The tool's `--live` path required an interactive TTY, so the repair was unrunnable from a session even against a throwaway tenant — and the prod report of 2026-09-05 showed the seven repairable rows all sitting in a `ux-audit-*` tenant, which the test-tenant policy already permits writing to. Two flags open exactly that door and no wider one, and neither restates the policy. **`--only-test-tenants`** resolves the slug of the tenant every TARGET row would RECEIVE — one `SELECT "slug" FROM "Tenant" WHERE "id" = $1` per DISTINCT proposed tenant, cached in `slugById` — and hands the WRITE LIST plus those slugs to the new pure guard **`assertTestTenantTargets(rows, slugById)`** in `scripts/lib/legacy-tenant-backfill.mjs`, which requires `isTestTenant(slug)` from `scripts/lib/test-tenants.cjs` (the file's own header already promised the CJS→ESM named-export interop this import relies on; `apps/api/scripts/*.mjs` has four precedents). ONE non-matching **or unresolvable** row refuses the WHOLE batch — **exit 3**, ZERO writes, every offender printed as `<table> <id> -> tenantId=… tenantSlug=…` on stdout AND stderr, and carried in `--json` as `testTenantError` + `summary.blocked`. The guard runs on the write list (a refused row is never written, so it cannot fail it) and in **every mode**, so the `--dry-run` the owner reads is the exact preflight of the live gate rather than a looser one. **`--confirm "<phrase>"`** supplies the typed confirmation as a value and is admissible ONLY alongside that guard — **exit 2** before any connection otherwise, with the rule named — and the phrase must equal `BACKFILL <n> ROWS` with `<n>` the applied count THIS invocation computed from its own classification, so a stale count is exit 3 with zero writes; it takes precedence over the jest-gated `BACKFILL_CONFIRM_TOKEN` (unchanged, still `JEST_WORKER_ID`-gated and loud) and relaxes neither `--backup-attested` nor the phrase check. **The orphan-user task is REFUSED under the guard (exit 2):** a NULL-tenant `User` is not tenant-scoped at all, so there is no slug to check and nothing the flag could promise — refused outright rather than silently ignored, which would let it LOOK as though a guard had run. Two side effects for the owner: every report line now carries **`tenantSlug=<slug>`** whenever the proposed tenant resolves (every mode, flag or not — one cached lookup, and it is precisely what the gate judges), and `--json` gains `onlyTestTenants` and is emitted from a single `emitJson` so the refusal path cannot drift from the normal one. Coverage: the unit spec goes 52 → **62 tests** — B8a–B8f on the pure guard (the approved set, one offender refusing the whole batch with a "1 of 2" count, an unresolvable id, an empty list, and the near-misses `testing-co`/`e2eclient`/bare `ux-audit` that must stay refused so the policy is never widened) and B5m–B5p at spawn level (`--confirm` without the guard → exit 2 with no connection attempt, the orphan refusal, the help text, and source pins on the slug query, the `assertTestTenantTargets(updates, slugById)` call site and the `--confirm`-over-token precedence) — and the DB lane goes 8 → **11 cases**: **D9** seeds a COMPLETE graph in a non-approved tenant (`acme-widgets-e2eguard`, created and deleted by the case; `assertTestTenant` is deliberately not called on it, being un-approved is the point, and `requireLocalDatabaseUrl` still refuses any non-local host) and proves exit 3 with the row still NULL in `--live` AND in `--dry-run`; **D10** proves a wrong `<n>` (`BACKFILL 99 ROWS`) is exit 3 with zero writes on a row the guard PASSES, so the count is a real check; **D11** drives the D7 production shape (a NULL-tenant `RouteRun` + two NULL-tenant stops) end to end with no TTY and no token — `--dry-run` shows `tenantSlug=` and `[1]` as the `RouteRun` statement of 3, `--live --only-test-tenants --confirm "BACKFILL 3 ROWS"` applies RouteRun 1 / RouteRunStop 2 in one transaction, and the re-run is "nothing to do". Gates on a quiet host (`node.exe` **8** on two consecutive 30 s checks): `jest --ci --testPathPatterns "backfill-legacy-tenant-ids"` **1 suite / 62 tests** (26.9 s); `npm run local:test:db` **6 suites / 37 tests** (77.9 s — backfill **D1–D11** green, 29.1 s for that suite alone); `tsc -p apps/api/tsconfig.build.json --noEmit` silent; `npm run lint -w apps/api` **0 errors** (7315 pre-existing warnings, unchanged in kind); `prettier --check` clean on all four changed files; `validate-lessons` **exit 0**, unchanged at 40/40 entries · 38.8/40.0 KB · archived 34 · nextId 79 (max L-078) — **no lessons entry**, this is a designed extension under an owner ruling, not a bug fix, and the register is AT its cap; `npm test -w apps/api -- --ci` **251 suites / 4089 tests** (333 s, +10 tests = the new unit cases, left fresh in `.campaign/runs/api.json`); `campaign-check` **exit 0** — 194 ledger rows across 31 shards, 81 affirmative claims, **0 undischarged**. Live-client scan of the added lines: 0 findings (the only slug-shaped literals are the policy's own approved names, the synthetic `acme-widgets-e2eguard` negative control and `e2e-backfill-*` fixtures). No new files — `fileCount` stays **1011**.
- **2026-09-05** — (worktree `rf-imp-E`, branch `feat/legacy-orphan-users-and-ocr-review`, off `origin/master` `17e81c2c` = #626; code commit `e848f19a`) — **THE BACKFILL TOOL LEARNS A SECOND, MUTUALLY EXCLUSIVE TASK (`--deactivate-orphan-users`), AND THE OCR GATE GETS ITS OWNER RULING.** Two independent follow-ons from the close-out, no schema change and no migration. **(1) Orphan users.** Everything the tool did until now repairs a NULL `tenantId` by DERIVING one from a parent row; a NULL-tenant `User` has no such parent, so it can never be backfilled at all. The prod census (2026-09-05) found six: three `SUPER_ADMIN`s, which are platform accounts and correct as they are, and three April-2026 `TENANT_ADMIN` leftovers that would 401 at login. **Owner decision 2026-09-05: those are DEACTIVATED, never deleted** — the row, its foreign keys and its audit trail all survive and the change is reversible by hand. The new task reads and writes ONLY `WHERE "tenantId" IS NULL AND "role" <> 'SUPER_ADMIN'`, writes only `User.status`, and never a `tenantId`. The inactive value is **`INACTIVE`**, read from `enum UserStatus { ACTIVE INACTIVE SUSPENDED }` in `apps/api/prisma/schema/tenancy.prisma` and mirrored by `UserStatus` in `packages/types/index.ts` (there is no `UserStatus` union in `packages/types/api/enums.ts` — that file does not carry it). Report line is `User <id> role=<role> status=<status> created=<t> updated=<t> -> <verdict>`: id, role, enum status and timestamps only, and the listing does not even SELECT `email`/`username`/`password`/`googleId`, so output discipline is enforced by the query rather than by remembering not to print things. Pure classifier `classifyOrphanUser({role, status, deletedAt}) → {verdict, status, reason}` over `ok` | `refuse: already inactive` | `refuse: super admin` | `refuse: deleted`, strongest-refusal-first so a row that trips two rules is reported by the one that matters (`refuse: super admin` is defensive — the listing's own WHERE already excludes the role, and it is refused AGAIN here so a future edit to the query cannot deactivate a platform account silently). `orphanUserUpdateSql()` is `UPDATE "User" SET "status" = $1, "updatedAt" = now() WHERE "id" = $2 AND "tenantId" IS NULL AND "role" <> 'SUPER_ADMIN' AND "status" = $3 RETURNING "id"` with `$1 = INACTIVE`, `$3 = ACTIVE` — every clause re-states a precondition the report displayed, which is exactly what makes a re-run a no-op and a row changed under us a rollback; `"updatedAt"` is set explicitly because Prisma's `@updatedAt` is client-side and does not fire on raw SQL. `--live` takes the same `--backup-attested` plus a typed `DEACTIVATE <n> USERS` (the jest-gated `BACKFILL_CONFIRM_TOKEN` applies unchanged). **The two tasks now share ONE write path**, the new `confirmAndApply(updates, phrase, applied, rollbackReason)` — confirmation, TTY check, `SET default_transaction_read_only = off`, `BEGIN`, per-row `RETURNING` check, rollback + **exit 4**, `COMMIT`, the `=== APPLIED ===` block — so neither task can grow a looser confirmation or an unchecked update by being edited alone; the tenant path's rollback wording ("was no longer NULL-tenant") is preserved verbatim through `rollbackReason`, and `--dry-run` printing is now `printStatements`, driven by `params.length` so the user task's third bound value prints without a second copy of the loop. **`--backfill-tenants` exists for exactly one reason:** the tenant backfill is the DEFAULT, so naming it explicitly is the only way to ASK for both — and asking for both is **exit 2** before any connection is opened, never a silent choice of one. `--json` gains a `task` field on both paths. Header comment, `--help` and `docs/IMPROVEMENTS.md` (one sentence, in the NULL-tenant follow-on's own paragraph) all carry the ruling. **(2) The OCR add-on gate**, `apps/api/src/billing/addon-gate-registry.ts`: the `ocr` row's `reviewBy` moves `2026-10-15` → **`2027-03-31`** and its `backfill` note is replaced with the owner's sentence verbatim — `Owner decision 2026-09-05: OCR available to all tenants; gate stays dark until OCR is monetized; no grants required`. The field names on `AddonGateEntry` really are `reviewBy` and `backfill`; `state` stays `dark`, so P1c (dark `reviewBy` not expired) and P1d (`ocr` is dark) both still pass and **`addon-gate-registry.spec.ts` needed no edit at all** — it never pinned the date or the note literally, only the `YYYY-MM-DD` shape and the expiry rule. Coverage: the unit spec goes 38 → **52 tests** (B6a–B6f every verdict branch plus the precedence, B7a–B7d `buildOrphanUserUpdates` + the exact statement + no `DELETE` and no `tenantId` write, B5i/B5j spawn-level exit 2 for the mutual exclusion and for `--live` without an attestation, B5k the help text, B5l source pins on the WHERE and on the SELECT list), and the DB lane gains **D8** — a NULL-tenant `TENANT_ADMIN`, a NULL-tenant `SUPER_ADMIN` and a tenanted `OPERATOR` are seeded under the run's `e2e-backfill-` prefix; only the first is listed at all (the other two never appear, because the WHERE hides them rather than refusing them), no `@example.invalid` address reaches stdout, `--dry-run` shows the one guarded statement with `$1 = INACTIVE / $3 = ACTIVE`, `--live` with `DEACTIVATE 1 USERS` sets it `INACTIVE` while leaving its `deletedAt`/`tenantId` and the other two rows untouched, and the re-run is "nothing to do"; teardown deletes `User` by prefix before the `Tenant`. Gates on a quiet host (`node.exe` **2** then **3** on two consecutive 30 s checks), jest cache cleared: unit spec **1 suite / 52 tests** (6.5 s); `jest --ci --testPathPatterns "(backfill-legacy-tenant-ids|addon-gate-registry|addon)"` **6 suites / 96 tests** (18.2 s); `jest --ci -c jest.repo-truth.config.js` **3 suites / 29 tests** (21.2 s); `npm run local:test:db` **6 suites / 34 tests** (21.8 s — backfill **D1–D8** all green); `tsc -p apps/api/tsconfig.build.json --noEmit` silent; `prettier --check` clean on all six changed files; `validate-lessons` **exit 0**, unchanged at 40/40 entries · 38.8/40.0 KB · archived 34 · nextId 79 (max L-078) — **no lessons entry**, because this is a designed extension and an owner ruling, not a bug fix, and the register is AT its 40-entry cap; `npm test -w apps/api -- --ci` **251 suites / 4079 tests** (363 s, +14 tests = the new unit cases, left fresh in `.campaign/runs/api.json`); `campaign-check` **exit 0** — 194 ledger rows across 31 shards, 81 affirmative claims, **0 undischarged**. Live-client scan of the added lines: 0 findings (no slug, business name, connection string, credential or UUID; the only address-shaped strings are the DB spec's synthetic `@example.invalid` fixtures). No new files — `fileCount` stays **1011**.

- **2026-09-05** — (worktree `rf-imp-E`, branch `fix/imp-closeout-review`) — **BACKFILL TOOL LEARNS ONE CASCADE LEVEL (`RouteRun` → `RouteRunStop`)**, on top of the close-out re-check fix round (base `origin/master` `1ebd4f54`, #623). The first read-only PROD report (2026-09-05) refused all seven NULL-`tenantId` rows, and the five `RouteRunStop`s for a single reason: their parent `RouteRun` rows are THEMSELVES NULL-tenant — two runs, one carrying 1 stop and one carrying 4 — even though each stop’s `RouteStop` and the run’s `Route` already name the same tenant, so classifying a stop against its run’s OWN `tenantId` refused every real row. `scripts/lib/legacy-tenant-backfill.mjs` gains **`classifyRouteRun({routeRowId, routeTenantId, stopTenantIds, stopCount})`**: the tenant comes from the `Route` (`refuse: parent missing` when that row is gone or its own `tenantId` is NULL), and the DISTINCT non-NULL `RouteStop.tenantId` values reached through the run’s stops are a CHECK, never a source — any mismatch is `refuse: parents disagree`, an empty set is fine (`array_agg` over zero rows is NULL, normalised). **`classifyRouteRunStop` gains `effectiveRunTenantId`** — the tenant THIS batch will write to a NULL-tenant run, supplied by the CLI only for runs whose own verdict is `ok`; the unchanged three-way rule then applies, so a stop under a REFUSED run stays `refuse: parent missing` and a disagreeing `RouteStop` is still refused (B1e–B1g pin exactly that). **`BACKFILL_TABLES` is now `["RouteRun","RouteRunStop","PaymentCounter","CreditNote"]` and that array IS the write order**: `buildUpdates` validates every `ok` report in one pass, then emits grouped by the whitelist, so no report ordering can make the batch write a child before its parent (B4f); `--live` executes both inside the SAME single transaction, rollback rule untouched. `scripts/backfill-legacy-tenant-ids.mjs` adds the `RouteRun` listing FIRST (it populates `ctx.repairedRunTenants`, which the stop listing reads), prints run rows with `routeId/routeRowId/routeTenantId/stopCount/stopTenantIds`, and tags a derived stop `(via run repaired in this batch)`; header, `--help` and `--json` all carry the fourth table. Coverage: the unit spec goes 28 → **38 tests** (B0a–B0e classifier, B1e–B1g effective tenant, B4f ordering, B5h CLI source pins) and the DB lane gains **D7** — a second NULL-tenant `RouteRun` with two NULL-tenant stops is reported `ok` + `ok (via run)`, `--dry-run` shows `[1]` as the `RouteRun` statement of 3, `--live` repairs all three in one transaction and the re-run is “nothing to do”. `docs/IMPROVEMENTS.md` now records what the prod report actually returned, including the two rows refused BY DESIGN: the literal `singleton` `PaymentCounter` (a pre-multi-tenant global counter) and a duplicate-numbered orphan `CreditNote` whose derived tenant would violate `@@unique([tenantId, creditNoteNumber])` — a human ruling, not a script’s. Gates on a quiet host (`node.exe` 3 on two consecutive 30 s checks), jest cache cleared: unit spec **38/38**, `local:test:db` **6 suites / 33 tests** (backfill D1–D7 green), `npm test -w apps/api --ci` **250 suites / 4043 tests**, `campaign-check` **0 undischarged** (194 rows / 31 shards / 81 claims), `validate-lessons` exit 0 (unchanged: 39/40, nextId 78), `prettier --check` clean, `tsc -p apps/api/tsconfig.build.json` clean. No new files and no lessons entry — this is a designed extension, not a bug fix; `fileCount` stays **1010**.

Three areas. **(1) `scripts/visibility-watchdog.mjs` is now actually bounded (L-077):** every `gh` call carries `timeout: 60_000, killSignal: "SIGKILL"` — the retry loop was bounded by attempt count only, so ONE hung call could outlive the whole public window; and the four test-only env overrides are routed through `testOverride()` and honoured ONLY inside a jest worker, loud either way, mirroring `SCHEMA_DRIFT_PRISMA_CLI`. **(2) Backfill tool proven against a real database:** the CreditNote listing selects `i."id" AS "invoiceRowId"` so a linked Invoice that is MISSING or itself NULL-tenant is `refuse: parent missing` instead of silently accepted off the Customer alone; a `buildUpdates` throw now blocks (not fails) report AND `--dry-run`, surfacing on stdout and as `batchError` + `summary.blocked` in `--json`; and a new DB-lane spec `apps/api/src/common/backfill-legacy-tenant-ids.db.spec.ts` executes report → `--dry-run` → `--live` → nothing-to-do → wrong-token → non-TTY against the compose Postgres, using a jest-gated `BACKFILL_CONFIRM_TOKEN` for the typed confirmation and refusing to run `--live` unless the ok set is exactly its own `e2e-backfill-` rows. **(3) Bookkeeping:** `turbo.json`'s `test:repo-truth` inputs gain `docker-compose.yml` and `turbo-inputs.spec.ts` pins all sixteen explicit inputs plus the lane's exact three specs; - **2026-09-04** — (worktree `rf-imp-04`, branch `refactor/imp-01-pricing-package`) — **CSP
  connect-src local-lane fix.** Root cause (proven by a Playwright trace): `next.config.mjs`'s
  `headers()` gated the `connect-src` dev-localhost relaxation on
  `isDev = NODE_ENV !== "production"`, which is always `false` in a **built** image (`next build`
  forces production) — so the local Docker/E2E lane's browser login (`POST
http://localhost:3000/api/v1/auth/login` from `http://localhost:3001`) was CSP-blocked with no
  HTTP response at all (`status -1`), and the login page's no-response fallback rendered it as
  "Invalid username or password." Fix: extracted the CSP construction into new
  `apps/web/csp.mjs` (`apiConnectSources(apiUrl)` + `buildContentSecurityPolicy({ isDev, apiUrl })`)
  and drive the http/ws relaxation off the **baked** `NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL` instead of `NODE_ENV` —
  an `http:` API origin now gets its concrete `http:`/`ws:` pair added to `connect-src` whenever
  the bundle was actually built with one, regardless of `isDev`; a normal `https://` prod build is
  byte-identical to before (pinned in `apps/web/lib/csp.test.ts`). Docs: `apps/web/e2e/LOCAL-LANE.md`.
  Lesson L (see `.claude/lessons/LESSONS.md` — deploy category): never gate a build-time artifact
  (CSP headers, routes manifest) on `NODE_ENV`; derive it from the build input it must match.

- **2026-09-03 (catalogue-lock coverage/order + lock liveness residuals + grammar/id/error-guard mop-up — the second refuter's 14 remaining items)** — (branch `feat/bug-registry`, PR #597; `scripts/campaign/bugs.mjs` + `scripts/campaign-check.mjs` + `scripts/campaign/reg-token.mjs` + `.gitignore` + SKILL.md, 14 fix commits, one per item; self-test 204 → 270 checks) — **THE CATALOGUE LOCK NOW COVERS EVERY WRITER, IN ONE ORDER, PROVEN BY A STATIC SOURCE SCAN.** (1) `move` read-modify-wrote `bugs.jsonl` inside its shard locks with NO catalogue lock — now `withCatalogueLock(() => withShardLocks(...))`, same one-way order as `file`; self-test races a stalled `file --batch F03` (deliberately a DIFFERENT batch than move's two shards, so the race exercises the catalogue and not an incidental shared shard lock) against `move B1 --to F02`. (2) `import`/`index` rewrote `bugs.jsonl` wholesale with NO lock at all — both wrapped in `withCatalogueLock`; self-test races `index` against a stalled `file`, proving a hand-written record with no catalogue row (so `file`'s own `expand()` call can't touch it) survives `file`'s later, stale-snapshot catalogue write. (3) `discharge`'s whole-ledger evidence scan read every OTHER shard under only its OWN shard lock, so two `discharge` calls on different batches never serialized — wrapped in `withCatalogueLock` BEFORE the shard lock (discharge is now exclusive repo-wide); self-test races `discharge(F01)` vs `discharge(F02)` (batches sharing no shard lock) with identical per-row evidence — the second is now refused, not silently admitted. (4) Lock order was never ASSERTED, only happened to be followed — new STATIC self-test parses this file's own source (locate each `withShardLock(`/`withShardLocks(` call's paren-matched span, fail if `withCatalogueLock(` appears inside it) plus a RUNTIME race of `move` against `discharge` asserting completion in <5s (a reversed-order deadlock costs ~10s+ per side); the order is now documented in the file header. LOCK LIVENESS: (5) a lockdir whose owner PID was reused by an unrelated LIVE process was never broken (`process.kill(pid,0)` answered forever) — `owner.json` now carries `bootAt` (`Date.now() - os.uptime()*1000`), and a waiter treats a pid whose `bootAt` doesn't match the CURRENT boot as dead outright, regardless of what the kill check says; a live pid on the SAME boot is still unbreakable. Lock directories are now gitignored (`.claude/campaign/**/*.lock/`, `.claude/campaign/bugs.jsonl.lock/`) — they sit inside the TRACKED `.claude/campaign/` tree. (6) a writer whose lock was stolen printed the warning and exited 0 — now sets `process.exitCode = 1`; the exit-code assertion needed `spawnSync` rather than the existing spawn+busy-poll pattern, since Node only populates a spawned child's own exit code once ITS event loop reaps it, which a synchronous busy-poll never yields for (verified empirically). (7) a lockdir crashed between `mkdir` and the owner write wedged every caller for 120s in 10s increments with no hint it self-heals — the owner write is now wrapped so a throw removes the directory it just created before rethrowing, and the give-up message names the 120s self-heal for an owner-less lockdir. GRAMMAR/ID: (8) the shared-grammar guard's team.mjs fragment (`"--paginate", "--slurp"`) didn't cover `.flat()` — tightened to the whole expression, plus an independent behavioural check against this file's own `flattenCommentPages`; mutation-probed (removing `.flat()` from team.mjs turns it red). (9) the token grammar (`REG_TOKEN_RE`) allows 4 digits but campaign-check's ROW-ID check was hard-coded to 1-3 — new `BUG_ID_RE` exported from `reg-token.mjs`, used at campaign-check's row-id check, bugs.mjs's commit-scan mention regex, and every `/^B\d+$/`-style id-argument check; a B1000 fixture row now passes the gate. (10) `status`/`brief` read `board.json` raw and could print `#[object Object]` — routed through the existing `boardIssue()` validator, same as `next`. ROLLBACK/CONTAINMENT: (11) `discharge`'s rollback restored the SHARD only — a record already written before a mid-batch failure kept `state: done`/`closed: yes` permanently; now collects `{path, before}` per record and restores each (records first, then the shard) on any throw; self-test uses a read-only (`chmodSync` 0o444) middle record to force the failure strictly between two successful writes. (12) `--build-plan` containment compared the path's TEXT, not the resolved target — a junction/symlink inside the repo could point anywhere and still read as repo-relative; now compares `realpathSync.native` of both sides (try/catch → fall back so the "does not exist" error still fires for a typo); self-test creates a real junction. (13) campaign-check labeled ANY byte-identical `dischargeEvidence` bucket "Grandfathered" and only warned — RULING: hard-coded `GRANDFATHERED_SHARED_EVIDENCE = new Set(["B24","B130","B154"])`, warns only when every id in a bucket is in that set, else fails; self-test plants a fresh duplicate outside the set (fails) alongside the historical pair (still warns). ERROR GUARDS: (14) campaign-check.mjs had no top-level guard (a raw stack trace on any IO fault, e.g. a shard path replaced by a directory) — body moved into `main()`, called inside a try/catch printing one `campaign-check: unexpected failure — …` line (`CAMPAIGN_CHECK_DEBUG=1` still shows the stack), same shape as bugs.mjs's own guard; separately, bugs.mjs's unknown-command guard consulted the PROTOTYPE CHAIN (`cmds["toString"]` etc. are truthy inherited functions on a plain object literal) — `cmds` is now `Object.create(null)`. Every fix ships with a self-test that drives the REAL command through the `BUGS_ROOT`/child-process seams and would have FAILED before the fix, and every one was MUTATION-PROBED (the fix reverted, confirmed red, restored) before commit.

- **2026-09-02 (mechanical remainder pass — the 9 items handed off after the concurrency/safety pass)** — (branch `feat/bug-registry`, PR #597; `scripts/campaign/bugs.mjs` + `scripts/campaign-check.mjs` + a new `scripts/campaign/reg-token.mjs` + SKILL.md, 8 fix commits, one per numbered item; self-test 181 → 204 checks) — **NOT lock/catalogue/occupancy/injection/pagination (those were already fixed by the prior pass) — the remaining mechanical minors/majors.** (1) `prove --build-plan` REJECTS an absolute or `../`-escaping path (`isAbsolute`, `relative(REPO_ROOT,…)`) instead of persisting it verbatim — an absolute path leaked a local username into the tracked shard and turned campaign-check red on every other machine; the normalised, forward-slash, repo-relative form is what gets stored now. (2) `discharge`'s byte-identical-evidence guard now seeds `seen` from every existing `dischargeEvidence` across ALL shards, not just the current call's rows — it is per-batch while the guard claims to be ledger-wide, so the same string reused across two separate `discharge` calls used to sail through both and campaign-check had to call the second one "grandfathered" instead of refusing it. (3) New `scripts/campaign/reg-token.mjs` (`REG_TOKEN_RE` = 1-4 digits, `hasRegToken`, `manualVerificationIds`/`hasManualVerificationRow`) replaces what a comment called "a literal duplicate" between `prove`'s T3 check (any digit count) and campaign-check's gate (exactly 2-3 digits) — a single-digit id could be proven but never discharged; the shared function also requires the token to sit in a TABLE ROW (`/^\s*\|/`) of the "## Manual verification" section after stripping fenced code blocks, identically on both sides — a token merely mentioned in prose, or inside a fence saying not to use it, no longer counts. (4) `file`'s catch now drops the ledger row it just added (new `dropLedgerRow`, under the shard lock) alongside the existing catalogue restore — the row used to survive as an ORPHAN with no catalogue entry and no record, invisible to every gate; a new self-test integrity check fails on any ledger row with no matching catalogue row. (5) `discharge` snapshots the shard file before its row loop and restores it whole on any throw — an IO failure mid-batch used to leave half the batch `done` and half `proven` with no rollback, and exit with a raw Node stack trace; the CLI entry point now wraps `cmds[cmd](rest)` so any exception `fail()` didn't anticipate prints one `bugs: unexpected failure in <cmd> — …` line instead (`BUGS_DEBUG=1` still shows the real stack). (6) `computeWaves` fills the holes in its sparse `waves` array with an explicit index loop instead of `.map`-ing over them (which SKIPS holes) — `waves[0]` used to serialise as `null`, not `[]`, whenever busy batches alone filled the agent cap. (7) `next`'s text output now prints EVERY wave-1 batch's own `claimCheck`, not just the head's — the rest of wave 1 used to be listed as "safe to run alongside it" with no warning even when their checks failed identically. (8) `selectBatches` tags each busy skip with `busyWhy` (`"in-flight"` | `"leased"`) so `blocked`'s reason renders `blocked by leased F##` when the holder is a remote `team.mjs` lease with no local in-flight row at all, instead of hardcoding "in-flight" for both. (9) INVESTIGATED, no code change: the earlier claim that `scripts/team/team.mjs` "gained a comment block only" is false — `git show 00d53223:scripts/team/team.mjs | npx prettier --check` fails, so master's copy was NOT prettier-clean and the dozens of reformat hunks in the diff are the pre-commit hook's doing, unavoidable on any touch that lands a commit (never bypass the hook to "fix" this). Every fix ships with a self-test that drives the REAL command through the existing `BUGS_ROOT`/child-process seams and would have FAILED before the fix (a real absolute/escaping/valid `--build-plan` path via `runCli`, a cross-batch evidence reuse via two real `discharge` calls, `hasRegToken`/`manualVerificationIds` agreement across digit counts and fence/prose/table-row placement, a forced `mkdirSync`-as-file failure that proves the ledger row is dropped alongside the catalogue, a forced record-directory failure mid-`discharge` that proves the whole-shard rollback and the clean `bugs:` message, `computeWaves` with the cap filled by busy batches asserting `waves[0]` deep-equals `[]` through `JSON.stringify`, `next` with two batches whose claim checks both fail, and a remote-lease-only occupancy fixture asserting the printed "blocked by leased F01").

- **2026-09-02 (concurrency + safety pass — answering the second refuter run)** — (branch `feat/bug-registry`, PR #597; `scripts/campaign/bugs.mjs` + `scripts/team/team.mjs` + SKILL.md, 5 fix commits, one per cluster of the adversarial pass that REFUTED the lock introduced in the note below, plus one test commit; self-test 129 → 181 checks) — **THE LOCK WAS BROKEN ON AGE, WHICH IS NOT LIVENESS — AND FOUR MORE.** (1) LOCK: `LOCK_STALE_MS = 5000` meant a lock older than 5s was rm'd and taken with no check that its holder was still running, so a LIVE writer whose critical section outlived 5s had its lock stolen and then wrote its stale snapshot back over two proven, evidence-backed rows — every process exiting 0, both gates green; worse, `releaseShardLock` and the exit hook rm'd the lock PATH unconditionally, so the stolen-from process went on to delete its SUCCESSOR's lock and admit a THIRD writer. A lock is now broken ONLY on its owner being DEAD: every lockdir carries `owner.json` = `{pid, token, at}` written immediately after the winning `mkdir`, a waiter breaks it when `process.kill(pid, 0)` reports ESRCH (which answers on win32 too), and AGE decides nothing except as a loud LAST RESORT (`LOCK_ABANDON_MS = 120000`) for a lockdir carrying no readable `owner.json` at all — a live pid is never broken on age, at any age. Release is identity-checked against that token: a process whose lock was broken leaves the directory alone and prints `bugs: our lock on F##.jsonl was broken by another process; verify the shard`. `LOCK_SPIN_MS` 2000 → 10000, because it was BELOW the old stale threshold — a waiter always gave up before it could reach the break, so a SIGKILLed writer's lock was never recovered in one invocation and the give-up message falsely blamed a running process. SIGINT/SIGTERM/SIGHUP are routed through `process.exit(130/143/129)`: Node's default action for those three skips `exit` listeners entirely, so a Ctrl-C mid-write used to leave the lockdir behind. (2) CATALOGUE RACE: `file` allocated its id from catalogue ∪ shards and wrote `bugs.jsonl` with NO lock at all, so two simultaneous `file --batch F01` both allocated B2, both exited 0 printing `filed B2`, and one entire bug — catalogue row, ledger row and record — vanished into a state so self-consistent that neither gate could see it. New `withCatalogueLock` over `bugs.jsonl.lock` uses the SAME primitive (a second hand-rolled copy is exactly how the two would drift) and `file` holds it from the id allocation THROUGH the catalogue write; the ledger write passes `{ mustBeNew: true }` so an id just allocated as fresh can never silently take the update branch. Lock order is one-way: catalogue, then shard. (3) CARVE-OUT OCCUPANCY: the sensitive test ran FIRST in `selectBatches` and `continue`d, so a parked batch that was in-flight or under a live lease never got `busy: true` — it occupied no slot of the agent cap, every hard conflict it carried was dropped from the colouring, and `next` offered the batch editing the SAME non-hub file to a second agent (reproduced from both the local in-flight path and a live lease). Parking is now a LABEL: busy-ness is computed BEFORE parking and a batch that is both lands in `parked` AND in the busy set that pre-colours wave 1. (4) SHELL INJECTION: `liveClaim` interpolated the `board.json` issue value into an `execSync` template string — on win32 that is cmd.exe, and a crafted board card EXECUTED. New `boardIssue()` validates at the boundary (`/^[1-9]d*$/` over the trimmed string; anything else falls into the honest `no board card for F##` branch, which also kills `next`'s `(issue #[object Object])` claim instruction), and the fetch moved to `execFileSync("gh", [...])` — no shell, ever. (5) PAGINATION BLINDNESS: both readers asked for `per_page=100` with no `--paginate`, so gh returned page 1 — the OLDEST 100 comments — while a claim is by construction the NEWEST; a lease posted as comment #101 was invisible to BOTH, and the batch occupied no capacity. Fixed in ONE commit in both files per the shared-grammar rule: `--paginate --slurp` with the array-of-pages flattened by the same expression (⚠️ `--slurp` cannot be combined with `--jq` — gh rejects the pair — so the projection happens in JS), and `readClaims` now carries each comment's server-assigned id and sorts ASCENDING so the LOWEST live claim wins, byte-mirroring `team.mjs`'s compare-and-swap (this file took API order and named a different holder). Every fix drives the REAL command through the `BUGS_ROOT`/child-process seams and would have FAILED before it: a live holder past the old 5s threshold whose waiter waits (both rows land, neither reverted), a SIGKILLed holder broken by the NEXT waiter in ONE invocation, a hand-written successor token that makes release refuse and leave the directory, a lockdir with no `owner.json` broken only as LAST RESORT, two concurrent `file` calls that must be given DISTINCT ids with both records on disk, a parked-and-busy batch from both the in-flight and lease directions, a hostile `board.json` whose marker never appears in any output, and a 101-comment fixture whose lease is only visible once the pages are joined. The `BUGS_TEST_STALL_MS` seam is now GATED on `BUGS_SELF_TEST=1` and capped at 30s — unguarded and uncapped it was honoured by every production ledger write inside the lock, i.e. a one-env-var route to the theft above rather than merely a slow write.

- **2026-09-02 (concurrency + scheduling pass)** — (branch `feat/bug-registry`, PR #597; `scripts/campaign/bugs.mjs` + `scripts/team/team.mjs` + SKILL.md, 3 code commits, one per blocker of the adversarial verify pass; self-test 107 → 129 checks) — **THREE DESIGN DEFECTS THE MECHANICAL PASS COULD NOT SEE, ALL OF THEM SILENT.** (1) THE SHARD LOCK: `upsertLedgerRow` was a read-modify-write with no lock, and the campaign runs sub-agents in parallel on one batch — two of them proving DIFFERENT rows of one `F##.jsonl` each read the whole shard and each wrote their own copy back, so the second erased the first; the verify pass reverted an already-proven, evidence-backed row to `queued` with BOTH gates green, because every write path asserts only its OWN row. Every shard read-modify-write now runs inside a `<shard>.lock` DIRECTORY (`withShardLock`, `mkdir` — atomic and loud on NTFS and POSIX, no new dependency), with the READ inside the hold or the caller's patch is already stale: `prove`/`discharge`/`reopen`/`claim`/`release`/`tier` each wrap their own read→judge→write region and `upsertLedgerRow` takes it again re-entrantly; `move` takes BOTH shards via `withShardLocks` in sorted order so two opposite moves cannot deadlock; a hold spins 2s at 20ms, breaks a lock older than 5s with a stderr note, and is released from a process `exit` handler as well as `finally` (`fail()` calls `process.exit`, which does not unwind). (2) WAVE OCCUPANCY: claiming a batch DELETED the constraints it carried — `computeWaves` coloured only `candidates` (every in-flight/leased batch already removed) and the conflict graph treated an `in-flight` row as CLOSED, so after claiming F11 `deps` no longer reported F11<->F12 and `next` offered F12 to a second agent editing the same two web files. A busy batch is now a PRE-COLOURED OCCUPANT of wave 1: it holds a slot of the agent cap, keeps its hard conflicts, and is never returned as a pick; `CONFLICTING` (= WORKABLE + `in-flight`) is what `batchConflicts` and `deps` filter on; `selectBatches` returns `busy` and `blocked`, `next` prints `blocked by in-flight F## — shares <file>` among its skipped reasons, and both `--json` payloads carry them — so wave 1 can legitimately be shorter than the cap, or empty. (3) CLAIM-GRAMMAR PARITY: `liveClaim` re-implemented `team.mjs`'s grammar and diverged on all three rules, every time the dangerous way — `--jq .[].body` flattened comment boundaries (destroying the marker filter), and `/^\s*(claim|release):/` allowed leading whitespace where team.mjs anchors `^claim:`/`^release:` with /m; one UNMARKED `claim:` comment shut the dispatcher down for every batch, and an unmarked OR indented `release:` freed a lease `team.mjs claim` still refuses with "held by …". Comments are now fetched structured (`--jq "[.[]|{body}]"`), parsed per COMMENT, marker-filtered before any parse, matched with team.mjs's own two regexes verbatim, and extracted as a pure `readClaims(comments)`; BOTH files carry a warning naming the other and listing the three rules — they must change together, in one commit. Every fix drives the REAL command and each was MUTATION-PROBED: removing the lock turns "BOTH rows landed" red, reverting either half of the occupancy fix turns 5 checks red, and restoring the old grammar turns exactly the three refuted cases red. New test seam `BUGS_TEST_STALL_MS` makes the lost-update race deterministic instead of hoped-for; the concurrency check drives two REAL child processes over `Promise.all(spawn)` via a small orchestrator, because the self-test is synchronous. || History in CHANGELOG.md (this directory). New sessions: add your note as a bullet at the top of CHANGELOG.md and REPLACE this field with it — never accumulate history here.

- **2026-09-02 (mechanical fix pass, post-review)** — (branch `feat/bug-registry`, PR #597; `scripts/campaign/bugs.mjs` + `scripts/campaign-check.mjs` + a new `scripts/campaign/normalize-evidence.mjs` + `.claude/hooks/stop.mjs` + SKILL.md, 14 commits, one per finding of a second refuter pass over the 7-lens review below) — **14 MORE DEFECTS CLOSED, LEDGER: `prove` now REQUIRES `--build-plan <path>` for a T3 row (resolved against the repo root, asserts the file's "## Manual verification" section carries the exact REG-id, persists `buildPlan`) — without it a T3 prove landed a claim campaign-check could never discharge; `move` now writes the destination shard BEFORE dropping the source (with an explicit `allowExistingIn` exception on `upsertLedgerRow`) and scans EVERY shard for a duplicate, not just `findShardOf`'s first hit — it used to drop the source on a refusal, destroying the authoritative row; `file` now writes the ledger row BEFORE the catalogue (id allocation is the union of catalogue + all shard ids) and restores the catalogue on any failure after — it used to write the catalogue first, so a refused concurrent write left a catalogue row describing a different bug than its own ledger row; campaign-check's T2 gate now falls back to a row's OWN `dischargeEvidence` when an existing web-e2e.json artifact has no hit for it (not just when the artifact is wholly absent) — a partial local Playwright run used to turn every OTHER already-discharged T2 row red; discharge's identical-evidence guard and campaign-check's shared-evidence warning now key on ONE normaliser (`normalize-evidence.mjs`: trim/collapse/lowercase) instead of raw vs `.trim()`'d comparisons; `writeRecord` now refreshes the header line itself on EVERY write (`reopen`/`prove`/`discharge`/`claim`/`release`/`tier` used to leave it stale until the next sync); `deferred` removed from campaign-check `VALID_STATES` (verified zero shards carried it — unrepresentable under one-row-per-id; `move` is the re-pointing operation). SYNC: the scan anchor is now returned by `commitMentions` and persisted by `cmds.sync` as its LAST statement, after every record has actually been written (was persisted BEFORE the per-record loop — a mid-run crash permanently lost events for bugs after the crash point, looking healthy); the two "an unscanned range" notes now go to stderr UNCONDITIONALLY regardless of `--quiet` (Gate 4's only mode), and stop.mjs's Gate 4 switched from `execSync`-based `sh()` to `spawnSync` so it can see stderr on a clean exit; `sync` now writes whenever the DERIVED front matter differs from the record's, not only when the body text does (a tier move never reached the record before); commit-subject hygiene — `appendHistory` strips `<!--`/`-->` from caller text (a poisoned commit subject pre-occupied a dedupe marker) and heals a hand-stripped marker instead of doubling the line; `git log` lines split on the FIRST tab only (a tabbed subject truncated); the "since the window" fallback no longer gets `.slice(0,8)`'d into "the wind". BRIEF/NEXT: brief's header counts WORKABLE rows (queued+regressed) with a `(N regressed)` breakdown and a regressed-batch banner + per-bug reopen citation (was "0 queued" hiding open work); `next --json` now emits `{next, alongside, eligible, waves, parked, skipped, cap, hubThreshold}` (was un-headed, no cap/hubThreshold); brief's "## Bugs" blocks now order by discovery.md's own `Ordering` backtick sequence when one exists, falling back to ledger order (new `BUGS_PIPELINE_DIR` override for testability); `liveClaim` returns `{unknown:true, why:"no board issue for this batch"}` instead of `null` for a batch with no board.json entry — `null` was indistinguishable from "checked, and free", so such a batch was silently offered with an uncompletable `claim <issue#>`. Every fix drives the REAL command (BUGS_ROOT/BUGS_SYNC_STATE/BUGS_PIPELINE_DIR seams + child-process refusal checks via `runCli`, now spawnable from a chdir'd throwaway git repo since `runCli` resolves its own script path via `import.meta.url` rather than `process.argv[1]`); self-test grew accordingly. || History in CHANGELOG.md (this directory). - **2026-09-02 (fix pass)** — (branch `feat/bug-registry`, PR #597; tooling + ledger/docs only, no app code) — **THE SEVEN-LENS REVIEW APPLIED: 30 commits, three workers, disjoint files.** Sonnet (mechanical): a filed `--symptom` now reaches the record's `## Reported evidence` and `index` PRESERVES undeclared catalogue fields (it had been destroying symptom/filedAt and 61 register PR links); the self-test now CALLS the shipped writers (`cmds.note`/`enrich`/`file`/`index`/`render`) against `BUGS_ROOT`-overridden throwaway dirs instead of re-implementing them (47 → 56 checks); `upsertLedgerRow` returns the RE-READ row and every caller asserts it; `--pr` validated as a positive integer, `flag()` rejects `--`-prefixed values, `normBatch()`/`normId()` at every boundary (B04 ≡ B4), duplicate guard across ALL shards; re-prove warns and keys History per PR; `enrich` bounded by the next heading; the body header line is REGENERATED from front matter on every refresh (B32/B211/six F14 records had drifted); readState fails loudly naming shard+line; Gate 4 checks the script exists and prints ONE stderr line on failure; new `tier`/`triage`; USAGE header lists all commands; a latent `ReferenceError` in `render`'s `inline()` that had never executed. Opus A (analysis): B34's refuted order-unpin rewritten as REFUTED with `contestedBy: B129`; B211 analysed from the code (it is a REAL critical — `reopenStop` accepts SKIPPED but the only client renders the control for COMPLETED only, web has none, so the strand has no in-product recovery); B32 → T1; `discovery.md` re-planned with PR1 = B129 + B211 and `trips.service.ts` added to the conflicts table; B129/B146 reconciled on the `getOrderTracking` split. Opus B (contracts): `sync` verifies EACH event it reports (revisited states no longer swallowed); commit scanner anchored on a persisted sha in `.claude/campaign/sync-state.json` + `(F##)` subjects fan out to the shard (`--rescan` for history); `discharge` requires PER-ROW evidence for T2 (three grandfathered rows warn); `waves` (greedy colouring, hard conflicts only, cap 4) and `next` returns the head of wave 1 excluding live claims / `in-flight`; `reopen` → `regressed` (added to campaign-check `VALID_STATES` with an evidence rule), `claim`/`release`; `brief` leads with `contestedBy`/`supersededBy`; `frontFor` no longer resurrects a proof `reopen` cleared; `tier` can CONFIRM a defaulted tier. ⚠️ `liveClaim` re-implements `team.mjs`'s claim grammar (no importable module) — change both in one commit. ⚠️ STILL OPEN, patch staged: `frontFor` derives `proof` from state so `already-fixed` B126/B127 assert a proof their ledger lacks; `file --batch` defaults tier T1 with no test plan. Honest E2 statement now in SKILL.md: automatic tracking = `prove`/`discharge` as the pipeline's exit, not commit archaeology (85 of 101 fix commits name neither a bug nor a batch). || History in CHANGELOG.md (this directory). - **2026-09-02** — (branch `feat/bug-registry`, PR #597; mechanical fix pass against the 7-lens
  review of the bug registry — 10 commits, `scripts/campaign/bugs.mjs` and `.claude/hooks/stop.mjs`
  only) — **THE REGISTER'S WRITE-VERIFIES-NOTHING FAMILY (L-067) CLOSED AT SIX MORE SITES.**
  `index` used to rebuild `bugs.jsonl` from a fixed 9-key shape and permanently destroy every
  filed `symptom`/`filedAt` plus 61 register PR links; it now spreads derived keys ON TOP of the
  existing row instead of replacing it, and never recomputes `register` from the ledger-derived
  `closed` field. `expand` now writes a filed bug's `--symptom` into a `## Reported evidence`
  section (same name `enrich` uses) so a filed record isn't six empty `_Not yet analysed._`
  sections with the description stranded in the catalogue alone. Extracted `replaceSection()` as
  the ONE section-replace helper `cmds.note` calls — the self-test used to assert against a hand
  copy of that regex, so a regression in the shipped writer couldn't fail it; it now calls the
  real `cmds.note`/`cmds.enrich`/`cmds.render`/`cmds.file`/`cmds.index` against a throwaway
  `BUGS_ROOT`/`BUGS_REGISTER`/`BUGS_RENDER_OUT` (all three now env-overridable). `enrich`'s
  section-replace terminator was bound to the literal `## History` heading, so it deleted every
  section in between when a different heading came first — now bounded by the next heading of any
  kind, verified by content, and appends when a record has no History heading at all instead of
  silently no-op'ing. **Ledger writes:** `prove` validates `--pr` as a positive integer BEFORE
  writing (`Number("x")` is NaN → silently wrote `"pr":null` while printing success); `flag()`
  rejects a value that starts with `--`; `upsertLedgerRow` now returns the RE-READ row and every
  caller (`file`/`prove`/`discharge`/`move`/new `tier`) asserts it before printing success, and its
  duplicate guard scans ALL shards, not just the target; a re-prove now warns and keys its History
  line on the PR number so it always logs. Added `normBatch()` (uppercase + `/^F\d{2}$/`) used by
  every batch-taking command including `file`, which was the one command that skipped it. Added
  `normId()`/`resolveId()` — "B04" and "B4" compare equal without migrating the nine zero-padded
  records — routed through `show`/`note`/`prove`/`move`/`brief`/`deps --bug` and `sync`'s
  commit-mention map. The body's `**Location** … **State**` header line was written ONCE at
  creation and never refreshed (B32 still said "Batch F11", B211 still said "State uncampaigned"
  long after the ledger moved on) — now regenerated from front matter on every `expand`/`sync`;
  ran `expand` once, corrected 9 already-drifted records. `readState`/`readShard` fail loudly
  naming the shard+line on malformed JSON instead of crashing opaquely; Gate 4 also checks the
  script exists and prints one stderr line (still never blocks) when `sync` fails. New commands:
  `tier <B###> <T1|T2|T3> --why` reconciles a ledger row's tier with what analysis concluded;
  `triage` lists the 17 catalogue bugs with no ledger row at all (invisible to
  next/status/deps/campaign-check). Also fixed: CRLF-terminated records silently lost their front
  matter (LF-only delimiter regex); `inline()` mangled snake_case identifiers inside code spans AND
  had a LATENT RUNTIME BUG the old self-test never exercised — unescaped `/**...**/` parsed as a
  block comment, leaving a bare `.replace(g, ...)` that threw `ReferenceError` the first time
  `render` actually ran; `render` now escapes severity/id before HTML interpolation; `file` now
  creates the ledger row BEFORE `expand()` (a fresh record used to bake in "uncampaigned" until an
  unrelated sync); `file --files` seeds `deps`' file set immediately. USAGE header now lists all 21
  commands (was 5). Full findings + skip rationale for out-of-scope items (SKILL.md, package.json,
  the sync event-report reliability issues explicitly owned by another worker, the carve-out
  classifier widening, the discharge evidence-pattern policy call) in the PR. Lesson **L-067**
  (escaping/round-trip pitfalls patching source via an intermediate script).

- **2026-09-02** — (branch `feat/bug-registry`, PR #597; tooling + ledger/docs only, no app code) — **IN-REPO BUG REGISTRY.** `.claude/campaign/bugs/B###.md` (211 per-bug records: derived front matter incl. a `files` list, six analysis sections, a `## Reported evidence` block imported from the register HTML, append-only History) + `scripts/campaign/bugs.mjs` (`file` `brief` `prove` `discharge` `move` `enrich` `deps` `render` `next` `status` `show` `note` `sync` `self-test` …). All ledger writes go through replace-in-place `upsertLedgerRow`. `sync` derives history from the proof ledger + git log and runs as **Gate 4 of `stop.mjs`** (reports, never blocks) — ⚠️ the hook exists on this branch only until #597 merges. `classify()` is the owner carve-out (money / tenancy / migration → planned, never auto-fixed; parks 16 of 19 queued batches). `deps` computes the conflict graph — ⚠️ hub files (orders.service.ts ×36, invoices.service.ts ×30, schema.prisma ×27) must be excluded or nothing is ever parallel-safe, and the graph cannot see method-level collisions inside a god-file (missed B34/B146 until the analysis file sets were merged in). F11 analysed by a 21-agent pass: every diagnosis held, every suggested fix was refuted on fix-correctness; B32 → F20; B211 (new Critical) filed. `bugs self-test` is a verify step because five write-path defects shipped from this file in one session (L-067). Seven-lens review (Opus/Sonnet) found 7 blockers / 23 majors — fix pass pending; see the review record in the PR.
- **2026-09-04 (2b, keepalive + spec hardening)** — `db-locks.ts`'s `lockPool(family)` now sizes
  **per family** (`POOL_MAX`: `cron` **12** for the monthly 7-holder peak plus a straggling hourly
  sweep, `order-merge` **8** unchanged) and sets `keepAlive: true` /
  `keepAliveInitialDelayMillis: 30_000` on BOTH pools — a lock connection is socket-idle for the
  whole hold (a cron tick's work runs on the Prisma pool), so an intermediate idle-reap would end
  the session, release the advisory lock mid-tick and let a second replica win an election for a
  running job (lesson L-075). `cron-lock.ts`'s header now states WHICH jobs self-repair a lost tick
  and which two do NOT (`tobacco-report.generateMonthlyReports` = `now − 1 month` only,
  `order-templates.generateDailyOrders` = today's weekday only; follow-on = catch-up windows), plus
  the hung-tick residual (a hold cap is deliberately rejected — it cannot cancel the body, so it
  would license two concurrent money ticks). Specs: `cron-lock.spec.ts` (f) now `fireOnTick()`s the
  REGISTERED job and asserts it went through the lock; `no-bare-cron.spec.ts` adds an
  import-specifier guard (spec files included — only `common/cron-lock.ts` may import the `Cron`
  identifier from `@nestjs/schedule`); `db-locks.spec.ts` (p) pins the per-family `max` and the
  keepalive options. `entitlements.service.ts`'s cache comment corrected: commission reconciliation
  DOES gate a write on a cached `hasFlag`, so the claim is bounded staleness, not "no write
  decision". `docs/IMPROVEMENTS.md` item 2/2b records both follow-ons.
- **2026-09-04 (2b, follow-up)** — `apps/api/src/common/db-locks.ts` now keeps **one `pg.Pool`
  per family** (`pools: Map<string, Pool>`, `lockPool(family)`, `max: 8` + 5s connect timeout
  each); new export `LOCK_FAMILIES = ["order-merge","cron"] as const` (+ `LockFamily`) is a closed
  allow-list `withAdvisoryLock` checks BEFORE connecting (`TypeError` otherwise), and
  `_resetLockPoolForTests()` ends and clears every pool. Rationale: a `@LeaderCron` winner pins a
  slot for its whole tick (≤ 7 at 02:00 UTC on the 1st), which out of one shared pool left order
  merges 1–3 slots and 503s. `@LeaderCron`'s skip lines are now
  `cron <name>: another instance holds the lock — tick skipped` (debug) and
  `cron <name>: lock connection unavailable — tick skipped` (warn), pinned by
  `cron-lock.spec.ts` (b)/(c); `db-locks.db.spec.ts` moved off its ad-hoc `t-lock` family onto
  `order-merge` (test-only KEYS) now that the family list is closed.

  wraps the tick in `withAdvisoryLock({family:"cron", key:name, mode:"try"})` (PR-2's helper) and
  THEN applies `@Cron(expr,{name})`, so `schedule.explorer` registers the WRAPPED method under a

The 4,438-line single file is gone; the datamodel is now seven files under `apps/api/prisma/schema/` — `_base` (datasource+generator), `tenancy` (15 models), `catalog` (11), `sales` (39), `finance` (28), `platform` (25), `compliance` (7) — 207 top-level blocks, 125 models, 80 enums, each enum placed beside the FIRST model that uses it. The split is a committed, idempotent script, `apps/api/scripts/split-prisma-schema.mjs`, driven by an explicit `MODEL_DOMAIN` name→file map: an unmapped model is a hard error (no "misc" bucket), so **adding a model is two edits — the domain file AND the map**. Its `--check` mode is the standing lossless guard: it re-parses the folder and proves the multiset of whitespace-normalized top-level blocks equals `git show HEAD:apps/api/prisma/schema.prisma`, plus the structural invariants (file set, unique names, `_base` holds no model, every model where the map says). - **2026-09-02** — (branch `fix/F11-run-cancel-skip-v2`, off master `c60fe214`; no schema change, no migration) — **F11 RUN CANCEL / SKIP RECONCILIATION (B129 Critical/B211/B146/B34).** Every cancelled run, and every run completed with a skipped stop, left its undelivered orders pinned to a stale `routeRunStopId` — invisible to the dispatch sweep and the trip builder (both require the pointer null), while the buyer card kept showing a driver and "you're next" for a called-off run. New private `routes.service.ts#releaseUndeliveredOrders(tx, stopIds)` (OUT_FOR_DELIVERY→CONFIRMED revert, then null both pointers for orders ∉ {DELIVERED, CANCELLED}) now runs inside the SAME transaction as both terminal writes: `updateRunStatus`'s CANCELLED/COMPLETED branch (in-tx read of the run's non-COMPLETED stop ids) and one line after the RF-016 auto-complete's `tx.routeRun.update` in both `completeStop` and `completeWithPayment`. SCHEDULED/IN_PROGRESS — including un-cancel — stays the bare update: un-cancel re-pins nothing and is not a path back to the released orders, since `createRun` refuses a route that already carries a SCHEDULED/IN_PROGRESS run - re-dispatch means cancelling again, then dispatching a fresh run. Every release call site logs its released count. `reopenStop` refuses `stop SKIPPED ∧ run COMPLETED` (the new state pair the release makes reachable) with a 400, decided before ownership and before any transaction; the REFUSAL is that state pair alone but the MESSAGE branches on whether the stop still holds orders, so a pre-deploy stranded row is pointed at the D4 repair instead of a trip builder that cannot see it. `findOneRun`'s legacy no-linked-orders fallback is skipped for any TERMINAL run - CANCELLED or COMPLETED (a run completed with every stop skipped releases its orders exactly like a cancel; without the guard the detail page shows those customers' CURRENT open orders as the run's contents). `orders.service.ts#getOrderTracking` returns `tracking: null` for a CANCELLED run's stop (checked right after the existing no-stop short-circuit); a SKIPPED own-stop keeps the full payload. **Sweep extraction (build-plan WP1(g)) was proposed and DROPPED by the lead** — `createRun`'s sweep stays byte-for-byte untouched (un-cancel is status-only, so it never gained a second caller). Mobile: `lib/skip-stop.ts#createSkipStopHandler` (pure; runId guard, in-flight guard, mutate-then-navigate, offline-queued counts as accepted) wired into the driver stop screen's Skip confirm via `useUpdateStopStatus` — before F11 that confirm was a bare `router.replace`, so Skip never sent a PATCH and the stop stayed PENDING (the `confirm-navigate` scan-ignore suppression covering exactly this is removed). `lib/order-tracking-logic.ts` gained `trackingHeadline` (cancelled > SKIPPED own-stop > IN_PROGRESS live-run copy > null) driving the buyer order screen's tracking line, and `trackingRefetchInterval` (now also polls while the run is IN_PROGRESS, not only while the order is OUT_FOR_DELIVERY/PARTIALLY_DELIVERED, so a CONFIRMED order can observe its own skip). Both mobile and web `useUpdate{RunStatus,RouteRunStatus}.onSuccess` invalidate `["orders"]` (web also adds `["trips-eligible-orders"]` — the trip-builder picker list — and `["trip-eligibility"]`) after a terminal write. D4 historical repair for pre-fix stranded rows: `scripts/repair-f11-stranded-orders.mjs` (dry-run by default, derived scope query, JSONL before-state, `REPAIR-RUNBOOK.md` §2). Specs: `routes.service.run-terminal-release{,.pins}.spec.ts`, `orders.service.tracking-contract{,.pins}.spec.ts`, mobile `__tests__/{order-tracking-logic,skip-stop,f11-run-cancel-skip.pins}.test.ts`. Lesson **L-045** (a pointer that gates re-entry must be released by every transition that makes the pointed-at thing terminal, inside that transition's own tx, on the durable forward-path marker — never a side row a payment-only path skips). Follow-ups filed (not F11's, allocate B212+): `RouteRunStop.skipReason` has zero writers/readers repo-wide; `updateStopStatus` stays non-transactional with an unvalidated inline body; only `completeWithPayment` writes `deliveredQty`; no run-row `FOR UPDATE`, whose lock order is also inverted against `completeStop`/`completeWithPayment` (a concurrent operator cancel and driver stop completion can deadlock); un-cancel leaves a run that can be neither refilled nor re-dispatched until it is cancelled again; `deleteRun`/B209 + cancel-while-unsettled policy.
- **2026-09-02** — (master `afce1a60`; #598 F14 + #599 quarantine + #600 discharge) — **F14 AUTHORIZATION / TENANCY MATRIX SHIPPED (B52 B132 B133 B138 B155 B165 B168).** **B52 (Critical)** — supplier-statement scans were readable CROSS-TENANT: the prefix had no `OWNER_LOOKUPS` entry so the owner check never ran. `uploads.controller.ts` now maps it AND the JWT path **defaults to deny before `path.join`/`fs.existsSync`**, so an unmapped prefix or flat key is refused rather than streamed and 403-vs-404 no longer leaks key existence; signed URLs and SUPER_ADMIN stay exempt. **B132/B133/B168** — DRIVER grants that outlived their screens removed from price overrides, order-template mutations and the four inventory WRITES (reads keep DRIVER, pinned); `addItem` now routes through a new `addItemForUser` ownership wrapper. **B165** — both JWT strategies propagate `impersonatedBy`, `AuditLog` is stamped with it, and `ImpersonationGuard` decodes the bearer payload directly because APP_GUARD ordering leaves `req.user` undefined where it runs. **B138** — impersonated logout clears the cookie and revokes NOTHING (an impersonation token's `sub` IS the tenant admin). **B155** — `refresh()` rotates the row IN PLACE via compare-and-swap on the old hash with a `count === 0` create fallback, so session identity and `createdAt` survive rotation. Proof: **210 suites / 3540 tests** (62 new), all 12 mutation targets probed. ⚠️ **The one that got past every Opus lens was caught by Fable's final pass:** spec 32's cleanup swept EVERY live session of the shared `admin` operator including `operator.json`'s. ⚠️ **And the post-deploy run still went red (33612887226)** — both new e2e projects are dependency-free, so phase 1 held them beside `auth-password` on that same shared user and spec 32's revokes took `AP-06` down; #599 **quarantined both project entries** (commented, not deleted) and run 33615066210 came back green with 11 steps EXECUTED. **Re-enabling needs a dedicated e2e user, never an ordering tweak.** Lessons **L-044** (green is a claim, not evidence — invert the test that asserts the old behaviour) and **L-050** (a spec that mutates shared auth state needs its own user). - **2026-09-02** — (branch `fix/F14-authz-matrix-v2`, off master `39632d27`; no schema change) — **F14 AUTHORIZATION / TENANCY MATRIX (B52 Critical/B132/B133/B138/B155/B165/B168).** Seven gaps across a single seam — a permission check that was declared somewhere OTHER than where the request actually flows. **B52 (Critical):** `uploads.controller.ts` gained an EIGHTH owner lookup (`supplier-statements/<scanId>/…`, B12's 2026-08-20 sweep missed it) plus a general **default-deny** — any key under a prefix with no owner lookup and no tenant-regex match now 403s BEFORE the filesystem read (fail-closed instead of B12's implicit allowlist, which silently streamed anything outside its seven owner-lookup prefixes); all three JWT-path denials now route through `denyFileAccess()`, which `logger.warn`s the branch, the key and the caller before throwing the UNCHANGED opaque 403 — nothing else logs a 403 (`SentryExceptionFilter` captures only `status >= 500`), so that line is the only signal when a legitimate read starts failing. **B132/B133/B168 — three unrelated DRIVER grants, same root cause (a decorator surviving the deletion of the screen that justified it):** `customers.controller.ts upsertCustomerPrice` DRIVER→gone (+ the service-level role gate HOISTED before any read, closing the implicit-delete-only gap the earlier B-fix left); `order-templates.controller.ts` DRIVER dropped from create/update/addItem/removeItem, `addItem` now delegates to a new ownership-aware `addItemForUser` wrapper (`findOneForUser` first); `inventory.controller.ts` DRIVER dropped from the 4 handler-level overrides that shadowed the class-level OPERATOR gate (`RolesGuard`'s `getAllAndOverride` always prefers the handler). **B165 — `impersonatedBy` reached nowhere:** minted onto impersonation tokens but dropped at BOTH `jwt.strategy.ts` and `buyer-jwt.strategy.ts`'s `validate()`, so it never reached `req.user`; now propagated, stamped onto every `AuditLog` row (`AuditService`/`AuditInterceptor`), and the `ImpersonationGuard` — which had been reading `request.user` for a claim that Nest's APP_GUARD ordering means is NEVER populated there (it runs before `JwtAuthGuard`), so its log line had literally never fired since it shipped — now decodes the bearer payload by hand instead. **B138:** `AuthController.logout` now short-circuits under impersonation (`user.impersonatedBy` truthy → clear cookie, return, never call `authService.logout`) — the pre-fix code revoked the IMPERSONATED TENANT_ADMIN's real refresh tokens on every device the instant a super-admin walked away, a live account-lockout bug. Web mirror: `lib/impersonation.ts` gained the single `exitImpersonation()` (banner + a NEW header avatar-menu conditional slot both call it — the menu no longer offers "Sign out" while impersonating, which is the client-side half of the same lockout). **B155:** `auth.service.ts refresh()` rotates the `RefreshToken` row IN PLACE (compare-and-swap `updateMany` on `{id, tokenHash}`) instead of delete-then-reissue, so Active Sessions shows a session's true `createdAt` across rotations instead of resetting it every ~15 min, and a client-cached session id survives rotation for revocation; race (`count:0`) and inactive-user (token still consumed before 401) behaviour preserved byte-for-byte. Web mirror: `SessionsCard` gained `data-session-id` (proves row identity survives rotation) + made `handleRevokeAll` honest (`Promise.allSettled`, never claims success on a partial failure) + re-syncs the list on any revoke failure instead of leaving a phantom row. `e2e-seed.js` seeds an ACTIVE `TENANT_ADMIN` (`e2e_admin`) B138's e2e precondition needs. **T2 (web) discharge is post-deploy, not here** (D1) — specs 31/32 + their `playwright.config.ts` project entries ship in this PR; `.claude/campaign/status/F14.jsonl` T1 rows → `proven` (campaign-check.mjs's actual `CLAIM_STATES`; build-plan.md's WP11 literally said `state: "fixed"`, which is not a member of that set — L-026 divergence, code wins), T2 rows → `proven-pending-deploy`.
