- **2026-09-01** — (branch `chore/validate-lessons`, off master `80bced16`; tooling only, no app code) — **LESSONS-REGISTER INTEGRITY AS A GATE.** New `scripts/validate-lessons.mjs` + `npm run validate-lessons`, wired as **step 2 of `npm run verify`** beside the lockfile gate (both are cheap file-only checks that must fail before turbo spends minutes). It closes the one class a merge cannot: `_meta.json`s `activeCount`/`archivedCount`/`nextId` are counts OF the files beside them, so when two branches each append an entry `LESSONS.md` merges CLEANLY — different `##` sections, no textual conflict — while BOTH sides' counts are individually correct and the total is neither. Observed live 2026-09-01: master 28, #588 +2 declaring 30, #590 +2 declaring 30, merged file 32. The only reason anyone looked is that `_meta.json` happened to conflict on adjacent lines. Six fatal classes: COUNT MISMATCH · DUPLICATE ID (within a file and ACROSS both — a compaction must MOVE an entry, not copy it) · NEXTID (must exceed the max id in EITHER file; an archived id is retired, never reissued, because the citation pointing at it still resolves) · **DANGLING REF** (a `[[L-0xx]]` naming no existing entry — the check that makes ids safely immutable, since renumbering breaks citations in the code map, the `new-feature` skill and CLAUDE.md silently) · CONFLICT MARKER · OVER CAP. Id-sequence gaps are deliberately NOT findings — a gap is normally an id reserved by an unmerged branch, and treating it as free is the exact mistake the register keeps paying for. All six classes mutation-proven to exit 1 against scratch copies, each with a self-explaining message naming the offending value. ⚠️ **The caps moved from constants into `_meta.json` (`maxEntries`/`maxBytes`)** so an owner ruling is a one-field edit rather than a code change — and they currently CONTRADICT each other: at the measured ~0.92 KB/entry the 40-entry cap implies ~37 KB against the 25 KB ceiling, so size really permits ~27 entries and the entry cap is unreachable. The gate reports that on every run rather than silently enforcing the stricter. ⚠️ **Master sits at 24.9/25.0 KB — 71 bytes of headroom, i.e. the NEXT entry anyone writes trips this gate**; that is the cap working, and the ruling it forces is the owner's. No lessons entry filed: this is a chore, not a fix — and adding one would itself breach the cap it enforces.
- **2026-09-01** — (branch `fix/mobile-google-signin-sdk55`, rebased onto master `3d1d8ea9`; no schema change) — **NATIVE GOOGLE SIGN-IN REPAIRED + FIVE NATIVE MODULES REALIGNED TO SDK 55** (the owner-approved publish-readiness scope, HANDOFF banner 2026-09-01). `(auth)/google-callback.tsx` destructured `{ default: SecureStore }` from two `await import("expo-secure-store")` calls; the package entry (`build/SecureStore.js`, verified in the installed 55.0.18 — zero `export default`, only named exports incl. the three this file calls) has **no default export**, so on native every token read/write ran on `undefined` and **Google Sign-In had never worked on any native build** — web unaffected via the localStorage branch, which is why it was invisible (L-025's untested-platform-branch class exactly). Both sites now bind the module namespace. Re-pinned to SDK 55's bundled versions (B203's crash class): `expo-location ~55.1.14`, `expo-task-manager ~55.0.20`, `expo-sharing ~55.0.24`, `@react-native-async-storage/async-storage 2.2.0`, `@react-native-community/netinfo 11.5.2` — ⚠️ **the spec's claim that root `overrides` pins three of these is WRONG**; it pins none (only the RN family + jest + sanitize-html + standardwebhooks), so the change is workspace-manifest-only and editing root would have ADDED pins nobody asked for (L-012 points the same way). ⚠️ Two of the five were pinned **AHEAD** of SDK 55, not behind (async-storage `^3.1.1`, netinfo `^12.0.1`) — these are deliberate downgrades; both still export a default at the pinned version, so their default imports stay correct, and tsc typechecks against the downgraded types clean. Deleted `react-native-worklets-core` (zero source hits, absent from `bundledNativeModules.json`, autolinked a SECOND JSI worklets runtime beside root-pinned `react-native-worklets@0.7.4`). Added `expo.install.exclude: ["jest","@types/react"]` so `expo install --check` can become a gate instead of exiting 1 forever. **Versioning is now REMOTE:** `app.json` `version 1.1.1` + `android.versionCode` DELETED, `eas.json` `appVersionSource: "remote"` — `local` + production `autoIncrement` was a duplicate-versionCode generator (cloud builds discard the local write, parallel worktrees can't see each other's counter, Play rejects a reused code); safe because `runtimeVersion.policy` is `appVersion`, not the incompatible `nativeVersion`. `app.config.js` needed no change (it spreads `config.android`). Lockfile regenerated with `npx -y npm@10.8.0` after catching local npm at **11.9.0** (first attempt discarded, lockfile restored from git) — `validate-lock` `missing 0`; all five resolve nested under `apps/mobile/node_modules` at the exact pins. ⚠️ **Across the #574 rebase the lockfile was never textually merged** — master's copy was taken whole, then regenerated from the merged manifest with the same pinned client; #574's four mobile bumps (`@react-navigation/bottom-tabs 7.18.18`, `@sentry/react 10.72.0`, `@tanstack/react-query 5.102.8`, `axios 1.20.0`) and all five SDK-55 pins coexist, verified in the regenerated lock rather than assumed. Preflight: `expo config --type introspect` exit 0 with `version 1.1.1` / `versionCode undefined` / `runtimeVersion appVersion` / `RECORD_AUDIO` still blocked; mobile tsc clean, eslint 0 errors, jest 105 suites / 1390 tests. ⚠️ **OWNER-BLOCKING and NOT done here:** `eas build:version:set -p android` only accepts an **interactive** prompt ("Input is required, but stdin is not readable"), and the remote counter is **UNSEEDED** ("does not have any versionCode configured") — a build before seeding gets versionCode 1, BELOW the installed 5, and installs as a downgrade. Then the Maps-key env paste and the `aapt2 dump xmltree … geo.API_KEY` proof (introspect shows the key resolving to `""` locally — the exact state in which the plugins silently strip it). ⚠️ Never `eas update` (inlines `apps/mobile/.env`'s LAN IP).
- **2026-09-01** — (branch `fix/F10-reopen-stop-state-guards`, off master `df1ef9a3`; no schema change) — **F10 REOPENSTOP AND STOP-STATE GUARDS (B54/B55/B71/B72/B120/B121).** One seam: the delivery stop/run state machine mutated money-, stock- and compliance-bearing state without guarding WHERE a transition came from or WHO made it. **B54 —** reopen's payment guard queried the legacy `transaction` model, which has **no writer anywhere in `apps/api/src`** (the live at-door writer is `InvoicePayment` via `recordDeliveryPaymentInTx`), so it always found zero rows and always passed: a paid delivery reopened freely, leaving a PAID invoice standing against an order the system then called undelivered, and the re-collection went unrecorded because `PAYABLE` excludes PAID. `reopenStop` now also reads `invoice.findFirst({orderId: {in: orderIds}, OR: [status in PAID/PARTIAL, payments: {some: CONFIRMED_PAYMENT}]})` and 400s **before the transaction opens**; the legacy guard **STAYS** so historical rows still block. Predicate is the shared `invoices/payment-predicates` const, never a literal (F03/F05 rule). **B55 —** the "compensating positive-qty SALE" loop was a **double-credit, not a reversal**: delivery writes no stock (`currentStock` is settled at order creation and re-settled only on item edit), so it inflated stock on every reopen and minted uncosted positive SALE rows into COGS. Deleted outright with nothing in its place; the conservation rule is now uniform — **creation decrements with no movement row, delivery writes nothing, reopen writes nothing.** ⚠️ Pre-fix damage is enumerable ONLY via those rows (`type:SALE`, `quantity>0`, `reference LIKE 'Reopen stop %'`), so that reference prefix is load-bearing for the D4 repair flight — never reuse it for a new writer. **B71 —** `updateStopStatus` had **no from-state guard at all**, so a COMPLETED stop could be PATCHed straight to SKIPPED, defeating `orders.service.ts`'s demotion guard (which keys on `stop.status === "COMPLETED"`) and stranding every side effect. Now 409s on a COMPLETED stop and under a COMPLETED/CANCELLED run, and loads the run for **every** caller (was driver-only). **B72 —** new `forbiddenRunTransition(from,to)` in `dto/update-run-status.dto.ts` is a deliberate **deny-list, not a whitelist**: only CANCELLED→COMPLETED and COMPLETED→SCHEDULED are refused; same→same stays legal for idempotent retries. **CANCELLED is deliberately NOT absorbing** (narrowed in review): with `deleteRun` refusing any run that recorded a delivery, `reopenStop` refusing cancelled runs, and the dispatch sweep requiring `routeRunStopId: null` (nothing clears it on cancel — F11's), a terminal CANCELLED would strand the run and its undelivered orders permanently, so CANCELLED→SCHEDULED/IN_PROGRESS stays legal as the recovery path; drivers are refused it in `updateRunStatus` ("ask your operator to restart it"), keeping the un-cancel an operator decision. Censused the callers first — web sends only CANCELLED, mobile sends IN_PROGRESS/COMPLETED, and an operator SCHEDULED→COMPLETED backfill is spec-pinned — a whitelist would have broken all three; COMPLETED→IN_PROGRESS is allowed **deliberately**. Ownership: DRIVER callers must match `run.driverId` in `updateRunStatus`, `completeStop` **and** `completeWithPayment` (the latter two fetched the driver row and never compared it), checked before POD ingest and before the tx opens so a hijacker's capture is never stored. **B120 —** reopen nulled `signatureUrl`/`podPhotoUrls` — the only pointers to real stored objects since #477 — with no deletion anywhere, orphaning regulated-delivery evidence irrecoverably. It now writes an `AuditLog` row (`action: "route_stop.reopened"`) carrying the discarded capture **inside the same transaction**, so evidence stays recoverable and objects stay referenced; storage is deliberately NOT deleted, and that row doubles as the forward reopen trail the pre-fix era never had. The stop reset in the same tx also appends `{archivedAt, signatureUrl, podPhotoUrls, reason:"reopen"}` to `RouteRunStop.podHistory`, the column F01 added for this write (audit row = who/when; `podHistory` = the stop-local archive a read surface joins to) — nothing is appended when a reopen displaces no pointers. **B121 —** `attachPodArtifact` took **no user parameter at all** and overwrote `signatureUrl` unconditionally for any new artifactId, letting any in-tenant caller substitute a completed regulated stop's compliance signature months later. Now takes `user: JwtPayload` (controller passes `@CurrentUser`), applies the driver-run binding **before** the idempotent read, and 409s a `kind:"signature"` write on a COMPLETED stop that already has one; photos stay appendable and the same-artifactId offline replay still returns the stored artifact. The sibling READ `getStopPod` (`GET .../pod`) gained the same `user` binding in the same pass — it presigns the signature and every POD photo, and `RouteRun.driverId` is mutable via `updateRun`, so leaving the read unbound would have let a reassigned driver keep pulling the compliance evidence the write guard had just closed. **NOT F10's:** the CANCEL path and `deleteRun` settlement gates remain F11's. Ships its D4 repair lane with the batch — `scripts/repair-f10-reopen-damage.mjs`; its scope is DERIVED (the pre-fix positive-SALE rows referencing `Reopen stop %`) rather than a hardcoded allowlist, and that prefix is load-bearing because those same rows are the only historical trace of a reopen. B54's stranded invoices are report-only: the money really was collected, so which record is wrong is a per-case owner judgment.
- **2026-09-01** — (branch `fix/F07-order-lifecycle-stock-conservation`, off master `df1ef9a3`, rebased onto `a5ad2b3c`; no schema change, no migration) — **F07 ORDER LIFECYCLE, STOCK CONSERVATION AND TEARDOWN (B10/B56/B64/B65/B105/B108/B116).** One root cause: the conservation rules were built for the _edit_ path and the _teardown_ paths were never enrolled in them — every signal each fix needed already sat in the same file, unconsulted. **B56:** `cancelImpact` reports `deliveredUnits` and `canCancel` additionally requires it be ≤ 0.001, so cancelling no longer voids the invoice for goods already delivered; the external-payment refusal is thrown first, byte-identical. **B64:** the CANCELLED branch credits the creation-time decrement back through the EXISTING `settleStockForEdit` (clamped to `max(0, qty − deliveredQty)`, same FOR UPDATE lock, **no StockMovement rows** — matches `create()`) and marks the lines it credited; `reopenOrder` re-takes exactly those. ⚠️ **Credit and mark are ONE decision** (`cancelReturnsStock`): the first implementation marked unconditionally, so a DRAFT cancel (correctly crediting nothing) still marked its lines and reopen then took stock the draft never held — a NEW conservation bug caught by review, now pinned by T7 and probe 3. **B65:** `deleteOrder` reverses each invoice regulated-ledger entry before deleting it — the invariant `voidInvoiceInTx`/`deleteInvoice` already held; OrdersModule now imports RegulatedModule, and that DI addition is why five untouched orders specs appear in the diff. **B105:** the DELIVERED auto-invoice is awaited with a 3-attempt Conflict retry and its failure reported to the operator naming the REAL control ("Generate Invoice (full order)" — review caught an invented "Create Invoice" label), raised AFTER the notification block so an invoicing failure never suppresses the customer delivery push. **B108:** `send`/`sendEmail` settle explicit credit intents BEFORE the oldest-first sweep — the swallow site comment claiming they catch up was FALSE, since both exclude exactly those credits; the exclusion is deliberately kept. **B116:** order-create stock is ONE aggregated, **tenant-scoped** set-based `UPDATE … FROM (VALUES …)` in a `{timeout:20000,maxWait:5000}` transaction (per-product aggregation load-bearing; raw SQL bypasses the tenant proxy the ORM call had). **B10:** the order-detail edit-closed banner renders for every `closedReason`, not only the buyer-only `DISPATCHED`; e2e spec 27 + its `playwright.config.ts` entry ship together. ⚠️ Cancel marks every line CANCELLED, so the web summary panel reads `summaryLineItems` or a cancelled order would report "Line items 0" beside real money totals. **9/9 mutation probes caught** (run by hand — the pipeline probe phase died on an account spend limit — with sha256-verified restores). Two out-of-scope instances of B65's invariant recorded not fixed: `routes.service.ts` (⚠️ **five** `order.updateMany` writers of `order.status`, only one runs the invoicing side effects → F11) and `customers.service.ts` purge (a FOURTH `reverseInvoiceEntries`-skipped hard delete — argues for a shared guard, not a fourth point-fix).
- **2026-09-01** — (branch `fix/multer-hoisted-override`, off master `a5ad2b3c`; no schema change) — **MULTER FIELD-PARSER DoS ACTUALLY MITIGATED (CVE-2026-82333 + CVE-2026-77078).** #574 bumped multer 2.2.0→2.3.0 and changed nothing: `@nestjs/platform-express@11.2.3` declares `multer` at an EXACT `2.2.0`, so the hoisted copy every FileInterceptor resolves stayed vulnerable while 2.3.0 sat nested under apps/api where nothing imports it. Root `overrides` now forces `multer ^2.3.0` — the documented L-012 exception (an override is the only lever past a parent's exact pin on a RUNTIME transitive; validate-lock treats it as informational `override-forced`, only a WORKSPACE break is fatal, and apps/api declares ^2.3.0). New **`common/upload-limits.ts`** is the single `limits` factory for all 16 interceptor sites across 9 controllers — `fieldArrayIndexLimit: 100` + `fieldNestingDepth: 5` beside the per-route fileSize, because 2.3.0 ships the CVE fix as an OPT-IN limit defaulting to Infinity. ⚠️ It returns a pre-built object with an INFERRED type on purpose: Nest declares its own closed 7-key `MulterOptions.limits` (NOT @types/multer) and @types/multer@2.2.0 is the newest published, so an inline literal is a TS2353 excess-property error — do not annotate the return type, do not cast. New **`common/multer-exception.filter.ts`** (registered LAST in main.ts; Nest reverses filter order) maps 2.3.0's newer codes to 400 — without it the guard answers 500 AND captures one Sentry event per attacker probe. ⚠️ **Lockfile procedure, learned the hard way (L-032):** an override applies only when npm RESOLVES an edge, so `--package-lock-only` silently no-ops; and deleting only the parent lock entry orphans `node_modules/multer/node_modules/*` — the nested `media-typer@0.3.0` vanished, `type-is@1.6.18` fell through to root `media-typer@1.1.0`, stopped recognising `multipart/form-data`, and EVERY upload silently returned 201 with no file. Correct procedure: remove the whole `node_modules/multer(/…)*` family, run a REAL `npx -y npm@10.8.2 install`, then assert hoisted==2.3.0 AND validate-lock `skew 0 new` AND a real multipart request still parses.

- **2026-09-01** — (branch `fix/customer-purge-ledger-reversal`, off master `a5ad2b3c`; no schema change) — **CUSTOMER PURGE REVERSES THE REGULATED LEDGER.** Fourth and last instance of the append-only-ledger invariant (`invoices.service` void/delete and `orders.service deleteOrder`/B65 already held it). All three invoice-destroying paths in `customers.service.ts` now call `reverseInvoiceEntries` per invoice before the deletes. ⚠️ **The reported site was not the reachable one:** `deleteCustomer`'s hard delete only runs when the pre-flight counted ZERO invoices (its reversal is defensive, closing the TOCTOU window against that out-of-tx count); the genuine leaks were `deleteAllCustomers` (blocks only PAID/SENT, so DRAFTs were destroyed) and `deleteImportedCustomers` (no invoice guard at all). Load-bearing fact: **a DRAFT invoice already carries ledger rows** — `createSplitInvoices` writes them in the transaction that creates the DRAFT — so a status filter here would reintroduce the leak. `CustomersModule` imports `RegulatedModule`; the DI addition ripples to nine specs. Spec `customers.purge-ledger.spec.ts` (4 tests, reversal-before-delete ordering with both call-log entries asserted present); mutation-probed. Lesson **L-031**: a reported location is a hypothesis — re-derive which sites can reach the bad state and sweep the file for siblings.
- **2026-09-01** — (branch `docs/train-close-out`, off master `02db5160`; no source-signature change, so no map entry moved) — **MERGE TRAIN + B167 DISCHARGE.** Eight PRs landed serially (D6): #580 (B167 e2e fix + F05 T1 flips), #582 (superseded plan), #584 (B167 → `done`), #581 (reconciled handoff), #579 (F17 close-out threads), #571 (lessons register + Gate 3), #574 (16 dep bumps), #585 (three fleet lessons). **Ledger now 193 rows — 46 `done` + 2 `already-fixed` = 48 terminal (24.9%), 145 queued, NOTHING mid-flight.** Source-visible changes are only `apps/web/e2e/23-run-settlement-note.spec.ts` (spec: fixture route renamed `E2E B167 Settlement <ts>` → `E2E B167 Run <ts>`, plus `exact: true` on the Settlement heading — specs carry no map entry) and the dependency versions in the three `apps/*/package.json` + `package-lock.json`. ⚠️ **#574's `multer` 2.2.0 → 2.3.0 bump is INEFFECTIVE at every call site:** `@nestjs/platform-express@11.2.3` declares `multer` at an EXACT `2.2.0`, so the hoisted `node_modules/multer` stays there and all 15 `FileInterceptor`/`FilesInterceptor` sites keep running it; 2.3.0 lands only nested under `apps/api`, reached solely by the two direct `memoryStorage` imports (`products.controller.ts:16`, `customers.controller.ts:18`). Two of multer's four `<2.3.0` advisories apply here (CVE-2026-82333 event-loop DoS — its fix is OPT-IN via `fieldArrayIndexLimit`; CVE-2026-77078 uncaught RangeError), two do not (all three `fileFilter`s are synchronous; the repo is `memoryStorage`-only). New pure **`orders/merge-items.ts`** — F30's `foldMergeItems`/`deriveUnitsPerBox` MOVED out of `orders.controller.ts` byte-identical (REG-B199 green UNMODIFIED) + `normalizeBoxUnawareSnapshots` and `normalizeBareIncomingSellingUnits`, because a box-UNAWARE line of a BOXED product states SELLING UNITS in `qty` while the fold adds a bare qty to `pieces`. **B47:** the buyer merge folds denomination-aware instead of summing `Number(li.qty)+item.qty` (2 boxes + a 1-box add billed 14 units at the BOX price, ~4.7x). **B51:** the CUSTOMER branch ignores client productId-null entries and preserves stored unlisted rows IN PLACE (deleteMany scoped to `productId not null`) — row ids survive, and it repairs a silent data loss where a buyer portal edit DROPPED operator-added unlisted lines. **B63:** a CUSTOMER items-edit on OUT_FOR_DELIVERY/PARTIALLY_DELIVERED/DELIVERED is refused 403 before ANY side effect; role-scoped, so DRIVER post-dispatch edits and CONFIRMED→PENDING are byte-identical. **B60 — the register premise was WRONG:** `loadActivePromotions` is `[]` for staff by design (P5-04), so the documented "PROMO billed at full price" case is unreachable; the real defect is the staff replaceAll block DESTROYING buyer-earned `promoFreeUnits`. It now preserves + rescales via `rescaleBogoFreeUnits`, ⚠️ **keyed to UNAMBIGUOUS pairs only** (one pre-edit line AND one incoming line per product) — the Fable final pass caught that a productId-keyed map handed the snapshot and the override attribution to a sibling line that never earned one (no-op save billed it 20.00 instead of 25.00); a replaceAll payload carries no line ids, so there is no sound N-to-N mapping and any other shape falls through rather than guessing. **B78:** `applyBuyerMergeHeader` carries notes (append) / urgent (only-sets-true) / requestedDeliveryDate onto the merged order before the sibling sweep; the DTO was deliberately NOT extended. **B62 (T2):** the order-edit page gates its add + substitute controls on `pricingReady` (customer queries settled; errored counts as ready so a failure cannot wedge the editor), MIRRORED onto the mobile operator edit-items screen — that half needs an Expo build. ⚠️ **Three residuals filed, not fixed, with reasons at the call sites:** a bare buyer qty for a product with NO stored line (re-split as PIECES by a pre-F06 clause — needs a client-contract decision), the STAFF scan-merge's unnormalized stored side (R12 froze it byte-identical), and the fold's price-survival contract being inert on the buyer path (the CUSTOMER branch re-prices and never reads `item.unitPrice`, so an operator MANUAL courtesy price does not survive a buyer merge — fix belongs in F07's region). - **2026-09-01** — (branch `chore/lessons-learned-system`, off master `b04f9a04`; no source, schema or map-entry change) — **LESSONS-LEARNED SYSTEM.** New in-repo register `.claude/lessons/` — `LESSONS.md` (23 seeded entries, capped at 40 / ~25 KB, grouped `process·tooling·testing·deploy·domain·security·perf`, each entry Symptom / Root cause / **Lesson** / Guard), `ARCHIVE.md` (overflow, append-only), `_meta.json` (`nextId`/`activeCount`/`updatedAt` — bookkeeping ONLY, per the ~90K-char `notes` incident this register now records as L-006). Entries were distilled from this profile's memory corpus, client identifiers scrubbed. **`.claude/hooks/stop.mjs` gained Gate 3** (lesson capture): blocks turn close when bug-fix-shaped work left `.claude/lessons/` untouched — (a) a `fix|hotfix|bugfix/*` branch with uncommitted source changes, (b) conventional `fix:` commits landed since the last commit touching `.claude/lessons/` (self-anchoring, same shape as Gate 2b). Any touch under `.claude/lessons/` silences it, including a bare `_meta.json.updatedAt` bump = the "no transferable lesson" acknowledgement. Gate 2 fires BEFORE Gate 3 on a code change, so isolating Gate 3 in a test needs the map touched too. Verified live on scratch branches: fix-branch block, acknowledge-escape unblock, `chore/*` no-false-positive, committed-`fix:`-drift block. CLAUDE.md gained a "Lessons learned routine" section; `bug-hunt` (register protocol step 5 — the register says what broke, the lessons file says what to do differently), `team` and `new-feature` point at it. Global side (outside this repo): `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` carries the same routine for ALL projects and a new `~/.claude/skills/lessons-learned/` skill bootstraps it on first contact with any other repo.
- **2026-08-31** — (branch `fix/F17-import-robustness`, merging master `4046e669`; no schema change) — **F17 IMPORT ROBUSTNESS (B98/B99/B112 + B08).** One class of defect: the importer trusted a raw primitive. New pure `import/parse-import-number.ts` — `parseImportNumber` (strips `$`/commas/spaces, **returns NaN for absent/non-numeric**) + `parseImportMoney` (+`roundMoney`) — replaces every bare `parseFloat` money/qty read in `import.service.ts`, because `parseFloat("1,234.56")` is **1** and $1,234.56 imported as $1.00 across four importers. ⚠️ The NaN return is load-bearing: `importInvoices` passes `|| "NaN"` as the Balance-Due sentinel and branches on `isNaN` to mean "no balance info — trust the Zoho label", so a 0-defaulting helper would flip those to PAID. ⚠️ Use `typeof raw !== "string"`, never `String(raw)` — `no-base-to-string` is an ESLint **error** and fails `verify`. `parseCsv` gained `bom: true` (Excel "CSV UTF-8" glues EF BB BF to the first header key and had been blanking column one). `importPayments` finally has the fallback dedupe its comment promised for rows with no `InvoicePayment ID`: matched against a **per-invoice snapshot taken BEFORE this run's inserts** (cent-equal amount + method + `createdAt` ±1 day), so a re-upload creates nothing while two identical legit rows in ONE file both import — and the match **consumes** its snapshot entry, or a partially-failed first upload would let one history row absorb both rows and lose a real payment. The snapshot excludes `"zoho-import"` placeholders and VOID rows (`importInvoices` mints that placeholder for PAID/PARTIAL invoices and deletes it just before each real create, so counting it would dedupe the first real payment away). Result is now `{imported, skipped, duplicates, errors}`, surfaced on `settings/import`. The post-import tenant-wide recalc was moved onto F03's `sumConfirmed` at `total - 0.001`; it had summed `status !== "VOID"` at `total - 0.01`, counting unconfirmed DRAFT money (B11's class) and able to silently undo a `repair-f17` repair on the next import. B08: the Migration Hub's Start button is disabled ("Connector coming soon") for `connected: false` sources — it used to start a job for Zoho/QuickBooks whose only connector is a stub that throws and is never injected. Repair lane `scripts/repair-f17.mjs` VOIDs the later twin of an exact-signature duplicate pair and recomputes that invoice's status (clusters >2 report-only; dry-run default, two flags to write, per-row CAS that **re-derives from freshly read totals+payments**, since re-checking only status/paidAt lets a concurrent payment turn a correct repair into a wrong one). ⚠️ `InvoicePayment` has **no `updatedAt` column** — a repair UPDATE that sets one fails. The `import-parsefloat-money` block was deleted from `scan-known-bugs.json`, so the signature gates fresh at 0 suppressed sites. Specs: `import-robustness.spec.ts` (REG-B98/B99/B112), e2e `25-migration-hub.spec.ts` (REG-B08) + `26-import-duplicates.spec.ts`, both with their own playwright project entries; ⚠️ neither self-skips on build age — on these surfaces a pre-change build and a regressed build are indistinguishable, so a skip would swallow the regression the spec exists to catch. **Deferred as B205:** the Migration Hub's OWN products-CSV path still carries B98's class (`parseProductsCsv` naive-splits on `,`; `MigrationService.optNum` does `Number(v)` → NaN). A fix was written here and REVERTED after adversarial review — routing `pricePerUnit` through a lenient parser turned a present-but-unparseable price from a loud `products.create` throw into a **silent $0.00 commit**, and `parseFloat` leniency admitted `-$5`/`12abc`on a path that calls`ProductsService.create()` in-process and so never runs the DTO. Lesson: a "same bug class" fix on a neighbouring surface is still scope creep, and a money fix that trades a loud failure for a silent one is a regression however correct its parsing is.
- **2026-09-01** — (branch `fix/F05-driver-at-door-money-settlement`, off master `26037bd4`; no schema change — F01's columns were already there and dead) — **F05 driver at-door money + run settlement SHIPPED (5 bugs: B49 Critical/B83/B148/B152/B167).** One cause: the route-run payload never carried the billed line money, so four driver screens re-derived `qty × unitPrice` and over-collected ~`unitsPerBox` on every boxed stop — and posted that figure as `payment.amount`. **G7** lands as `RUN_LINE_ITEMS_SELECT` (one const for all three run-read sites, now carrying subtotal/boxes/pieces/unitsPerBox) — **F10/F11/F12/F22 consume it; extend, never re-inline.** Mobile money moves into pure `lib/run-money.ts` (Decimals arrive as STRINGS). **B148 was the reachability blocker:** the mobile payload always sent `deliveries[].productId`, the DTO never declared it, and the global `forbidNonWhitelisted` pipe 400'd _every_ stop completion — the DTO now takes it and both mutation-create sites persist it (which also un-deads `reopenStop`'s productId-gated stock restore). **B83:** `recordDeliveryPaymentInTx` books its unapplied residual as an `AdvancePayment` (single-customer guarded, `RUN:<runId>:STOP:<stopId>` reference — a load-bearing token the cash-truth helper matches by prefix) instead of warn-and-drop; the zero-payable-invoice path that discarded the WHOLE amount is absorbed too. **B152/B167:** settlement is server-owned — `POST :id/settlement` writes F01's dead `settlementNote`/`settlementVariance` columns with a server-computed expected, and the web run-detail page renders them read-only for every status. ⚠️ **The register missed the real bypass:** the RF-016 auto-complete inside `completeStop`/`completeWithPayment` flips the run COMPLETED in its own tx, so a backstop in `updateRunStatus` alone never fires on the common path — all three paths are gated. ⚠️ The Fable final pass caught that CANCELLED runs could strand cash unsettleable forever (settleRun now accepts them post-hoc) and that the e2e variance assertion was satisfied by the note text (now scoped to the badge testid). **Cancel-path and `deleteRun` gating handed to F11.**
- **2026-08-31** — (branch `fix/F03-payment-truth`, merging master `6a0ef8fa`) — **F03 payment-status truth SHIPPED (9 bugs: B11/B57/B74/B81/B84/B85/B97/B102/B103).** `payment-predicates.ts` is the single source of truth; every payment SUM narrows to PAID while every LISTING keeps all rows. Scope widened at review past the original three-function fence, which was itself the defect (same tenant read $200 on /bookkeeping/summary vs $500 on /bookkeeping/dashboard); also routed customers, buyer statements and payment-requests. `getCashFlow`/`getPaymentsReceivedReport` byte-untouched as the reference reads. `RECORDED` fixtures corrected to `PAID` (the enum is DRAFT|PAID|VOID — RECORDED was masking three unrouted sites). T-B50s pins the server oracle cap, mutation-verified by hand. e2e 22-payment-truth wired into playwright.config.ts — the third spec that nearly shipped with no projects entry and would never have run.
- **2026-08-31** — (branch `fix/mobile-native-filesystem-crash`, off master `26037bd4`; no schema change) — **B203/B204: THE FIRST APK EVER RUN EXPOSED TWO NATIVE-ONLY LAUNCH BLOCKERS.** B203: `expo-file-system` was pinned `~18.1.11` against SDK 55's `~55.0.26` — its native module predates `FilePermissionModule`, so expo-modules-core 55 crashed the app pre-UI (`NoClassDefFoundError`); the pin protected the legacy API used only by `lib/share-pdf.ts`, which now imports `expo-file-system/legacy` (⚠️ npm kept a stale nested 18.1.11 it itself called `invalid` — deleted from tree AND lockfile). B204: expo-secure-store validates keys against `/^[\w.-]+$/` on reads and writes; every storage key is colon-namespaced, so on device every session read/write THREW — `buyer-session-store.initialize` (the one boot store with no catch) froze the app on the splash spinner forever, and even unfrozen no login could persist. Fix: pure `lib/secure-key.ts` mapper (`:`→`_`) on the NATIVE branch of all 22 SecureStore call sites across 8 files (web keys untouched — nobody signed out; zero native installs — no migration) + try/finally in the buyer gate, pinned RED-first. ⚠️ Verified on-emulator: post-fix logcat shows ExpoModulesCore ✅ init + `Running "main"`, no FATAL.

- **2026-08-31** — (branch `build/mobile-eas-android-ota`, off master `e2bc8b33`; no schema change) — **THE FIRST EAS CONFIG THAT CAN PRODUCE AN INSTALLABLE ANDROID BUILD, + OTA.** Context: the only way a client has ever run this app is mobile web; the one native build ever attempted (2026-04-20) died in EAS's Install-dependencies phase in 8.5s with no artifact. `app.json` gains config plugins for `expo-camera`/`expo-location`/`expo-image-picker` — all three were dependencies with NO plugin, so a native build shipped without their permissions (fatal for a scan retest; and ⚠️ `expo-location` needs `isAndroidBackgroundLocationEnabled`+`isAndroidForegroundServiceEnabled` or driver tracking degrades silently) — plus ⚠️ `android.blockedPermissions: [RECORD_AUDIO]`, which is load-bearing because the camera plugin's `recordAudioAndroid:false` only stops the PLUGIN adding it while expo-camera's own library manifest re-adds it. OTA: `expo-updates` + `updates.url` + `runtimeVersion.policy=appVersion` (⚠️ NOT fingerprint — `app.config.js` injects a Maps key into the react-native-maps plugin, so fingerprints differ by environment and updates strand; ⚠️ and it must be IN a binary for that binary to ever receive an update). `eas.json`: `staging` = the retest profile (internal+apk+prod API) with `channel`, all profiles `node: 20.19.4` (the lever that fixes the builder's bundled npm to 10.8.x) and `HUSKY: 0` (root `prepare: husky` on an archive with no `.git` — leading suspect for the April failure). Deleted the stale conflicting repo-root `eas.json`/`app.json` (different projectId AND bundleId, referenced by nothing). ⚠️ `expo prebuild` rewrites package.json's android/ios scripts to bare-workflow `expo run:*` — revert them, and never commit `android/`. Preflighted: config resolves, prebuild manifest carries CAMERA/ACCESS_BACKGROUND_LOCATION/FOREGROUND_SERVICE_LOCATION with RECORD_AUDIO `tools:node=remove`, expo-doctor shows no NEW failures (same 4 fail on clean master), web export still builds, validate-lock `missing 0`, jest 1366 green.

- **2026-08-31** — (branch `fix/B202-web-scan-engine`, off master `45c857ac`; no schema change) — **REG-B202: PORT THE F30 SCAN ENGINE INTO THE WEB CAMERA PATH (+2 operator riders).** `ScanCamera.web.tsx` was deliberately skipped by F30 and still had three defects native removed: decode starvation (its rAF loop awaited the FULL resolve before releasing `inFlightRef`, so no frame was decoded mid-resolve — buffering fixes nothing if the buffer is never offered a code), drop-not-queue (`fire()`'s `busyRef` bailed before the gate — REG-B192 verbatim), and a legacy re-anchor (`gateRef.current = {lastCode, lastAt}` on every settle, pinning the gate to the single-slot shape). NEW pure `lib/scan-engine.ts` (`frameScanned`/`manualScanned`/`scanSettled` → `{next, startResolving, indicator}`) sequences the shipped `scan-loop.ts` gate and `scan-pending-buffer.ts` buffer with NO policy of its own; `ScanCamera.web.tsx` is now a thin adapter whose decode loop calls a SYNCHRONOUS `handleFrame` and releases `inFlightRef` on the DECODE settling, plus a recursive `resolveCode` drain and the `onResolvingChange` prop that lights `ScanOrderSheet`'s "Looking up…". ⚠️ A `manualScanned` that consults the gate is the escape a state-shape-only test misses — the pin seeds a real cooldown and asserts the typed code still lands. RIDERS: (1) web accept cue — `lib/scan-feedback.ts` (pure decision) + `lib/scan-cue{,.web}.ts` (WebAudio blip + vibrate, both TOTAL); web had NO scan confirmation at all since `lib/haptics.ts`'s `scanHaptic` early-returns there. ⚠️ Its totality test is VACUOUS in bare node (no `window` ⇒ beep returns early, `navigator.vibrate?.()` optional-chains) — pins install a throwing AudioContext. (2) login-screen build stamp — `lib/build-info.ts` + Dockerfile `ARG/ENV EXPO_PUBLIC_BUILD_SHA` (falls back to `RAILWAY_GIT_COMMIT_SHA`, forwarding UNVERIFIED — if a deploy reads "build dev", add the service variable); inlining verified against a real web export. ⚠️ Local `expo export --platform web` needs `NODE_PATH=$(pwd)/node_modules` (the expo-router resolution class the Dockerfile already pins). Mobile suite 103 suites / 1366 tests green; tsc/eslint/prettier clean.
- **2026-08-31** — (branch `fix/B199-merge-lock-scope-decision`, off master `da8a61b6`; no schema change) — **B199 merge-lock scope: DEFERRED ON EVIDENCE, not left dangling.** `@routeflow/api` runs exactly ONE instance — no `numReplicas` in `apps/api/railway.toml`, `numReplicas = null` for every service in Railway's API (no dashboard override), live deployment reports 1 running instance — so `withOrderMergeLock`'s per-process scope is sufficient and the cross-replica lost update is UNREACHABLE. Restructuring `updateOrderItems` (money-critical) to close an unreachable race was judged the larger risk. ⚠️ The trigger is SCALING and it is SILENT — wrong money with no error, log or failing test — and Railway injects no replica-count variable, so nothing in-process can detect it; the guard is therefore a comment in `apps/api/railway.toml`'s `[deploy]` block, at the point the decision is made. Three designs recorded in `withOrderMergeLock`'s doc comment, cheapest first: Redis lock (Redis already a dependency, smallest diff, keeps the T-B199 spec's one-instance framing valid), optimistic CAS + 409 retry, then the full fold-inside-the-transaction (which requires MOVING, never deleting, the spec's "two concurrent merges on ONE INSTANCE serialize" block).

- **2026-08-31** — (master `88fd3a3f`; CAMPAIGN PAUSED by the owner) — map pass for the E2E heal #556 only: `web.md` 02-operator entry records why OP-09c/OP-11b must never target "the first row" (spec 21 guard fixtures the guard itself refuses to delete sort into row 1; OP-09c wrote 9.75 onto a $1.00-list leftover, the tier-monotonicity glyph joined the label text node and killed the exact-match locator — the chronic deploy-signal red). Both now resolve a stable seeded product by API and match /^Tier 2/. ⚠️ DELIBERATELY UNMAPPED: the 24 files the Stop gate flags under `fix/F03-payment-truth` are UNMERGED WIP (commit d1269474) plus an isolated, droppable repo-wide prettier pass (80b75ff9) — the map tracks master, and F03 gets its entries when it merges, not before.
- **2026-08-31** — (branch `fix/F30-scan-loss`, worktree `.claude/worktrees/rf-F30`, off master `e7eb1627`; **SCHEMA CHANGE — migration `20260910000000_order_idempotency`, additive only, prod-apply BEFORE the merge**) — **F30: the "scanned items go missing / land twice" cluster (B190–B201), mobile client + API server half in ONE PR.** Mobile: `lib/scan-loop.ts`'s gate is now a **4-slot LRU with per-code clocks** — new `ScanSlot{code,lastAcceptAt,lastSeenAt}` + `slots?`, `SCAN_SLOTS`=4, `ABSENCE_GAP_MS`=300, with `lastAt`/`lastAcceptAt`/`lastSeenAt` optional top-level MIRRORS of `slots[0]` so legacy `{lastCode,lastAt}` callers (`ScanCamera.web.tsx`) still compile and an external write re-anchors the head. ⚠️ **`lastAcceptAt` moves only on ACCEPT** (refreshing it on a rejected per-frame repeat swallows a deliberate re-scan = REG-B191) while `lastSeenAt` moves every frame; slots match on intersecting `normalizeScanCode` candidate SETS, and one slot was never enough because a carton printed with two genuinely different codes (an ITF-14 case code with a non-zero packaging indicator does NOT normalize to its inner EAN-13) reset it on every A→B flip — REG-B190's runaway "N cs + M loose". NEW pure libs: `lib/scan-pending-buffer.ts` (depth-2 deduped queue replacing `ScanCamera`'s drop-not-queue `busyRef`; `ScanCamera` gained `onResolvingChange` and drains by recursing after `onScanned` settles → `ScanOrderSheet`'s "looking up…"), `lib/wedge-submit.ts` (`createWedgeSubmitHandler` clears the field SYNCHRONOUSLY at submit per web's `CreateOrderModal` invariant and buffers a mid-resolve burst instead of concatenating it; `createAutoAddGuard`/`createScanAttempt` make a per-scan ATTEMPT — not a time window — decide whether a settle may auto-add, REG-B201), `lib/queue-drain.ts` (`drainQueue`/`buildReplayRequestConfig`/`MAX_RETRIES` — the ONE place that classifies delivered vs retry vs failure), `lib/toast-host.ts` (`register`/`release`/`getToastHost`; `lib/toast.ts`'s iOS branch is no longer a no-op — it routes to the mounted `InlineToast`, Alert fallback, and registration lives in `useInlineToast` itself so no screen can forget it, REG-B151), `lib/order-submit-key.ts` (one uuid per CART SESSION, surviving every failed attempt, reset only when a submit lands). `lib/scan-ladder.ts` caps a lookup at `SCAN_RESOLVE_TIMEOUT_MS`=5000 and **ABORTS** rather than races (⚠️ a `Promise.race` discards only the VALUE — the ladder keeps running and `deps.accept` writes to the cart after the operator was told "try again"); `deps.resolve` is now `(code, signal?)`. `lib/barcode-resolve.ts` gained a distinct **`BarcodeResolveArchived`** variant + `archivedMessage()`: both rungs dropped their `isActive` filter so they agree (search `limit` 10→20 to compensate), the ambiguity `matches` list is SELLABLE-only, and ⚠️ **every COMMITTING caller must branch on `archived`** (scan-ladder, driver at-door adjust, stock-count, vendor-bills, `ProductPickerSheet`) while navigate-only/read-only callers deliberately treat it as an ordinary hit — reaching an archived product's screen is how it gets reactivated. `lib/sale-line.ts` (money-critical): new private `splitFromPrev`/`piecesFromPrev` — an EXPLICIT boxes/pieces split is returned verbatim, but a typed PLAIN qty on a boxed product folds through `normalizeBoxesPieces` instead of reading as an empty line (REG-B194), and increment/decrement/piece all derive from it. `lib/order-draft-logic.ts` `decideResumeLine` keeps a parked line **including archived** (`/products/:id` returns inactive rows the catalog list hides), drops only on 404, and aborts the WHOLE hydration on any transient failure so autosave can't PATCH a truncated cart. Offline queue is never silent (REG-B143/B111): 4xx/retry-exhausted entries land in persisted `offlineQueue.failedActions` (capped `MAX_FAILED_ACTIONS`=50, `selectFailedActionCount`) + `alertInfo`, surfaced by NEW `components/OfflineBanner.tsx` mounted in both `(driver)/_layout.tsx` and `(operator)/_layout.tsx`; ⚠️ `lib/api-client.ts` only auto-enqueues when NetInfo actually says OFFLINE — a 15s `ECONNABORTED` on a live connection is no longer classified as offline (that queued a duplicate of a write the server may already have applied), and replays carry `_offlineQueued` + the replay-safe `idempotency-key` header. API: `Order.idempotencyKey String?` + `@@unique([tenantId, idempotencyKey])` (NULLs distinct ⇒ no backfill, old clients unaffected); `POST /orders` reads an **`Idempotency-Key` HEADER, never a body field**, P2002 on that unique = replay the ORIGINAL order, and ⚠️ **the staff auto-merge branch in the CONTROLLER returns without ever reaching `create()`**, so it checks/stamps the key itself and only AFTER the fold landed. The read-fold-write critical section runs under new module-private `withOrderMergeLock(orderId, fn)`/`mergeLocksByOrder` on the controller, chaining `fn` onto the prior turn's SETTLED promise so two merges racing one order can't both read the same pre-write snapshot and clobber each other's delta (the lost-update half of B199) — **scope RESOLVED as a deliberate DEFERRAL 2026-08-31, see the bullet at the top of this file: the api service runs exactly 1 replica (verified 3 ways), so the cross-replica half is unreachable; the guard is a comment in apps/api/railway.toml's [deploy] block and the three fix designs are in withOrderMergeLock's doc comment**; `fn` re-reads `findActiveOrder` INSIDE the lock because the pre-lock snapshot is stale by the time a queued turn runs. ⚠️ That Map is per controller INSTANCE — it serializes ONE API process, so two replicas still race their reads (`updateOrderItems`' `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` serializes the WRITE, not the read the absolute totals came from); the full fix is folding inside `updateOrderItems`' own transaction, tracked and deliberately not done here. New module-private `foldMergeItems`/`deriveUnitsPerBox` fold a merge denomination-aware (boxes with boxes, pieces with pieces, `normalizeBoxesPieces` re-derives; ⚠️ a null `OrderItem.unitsPerBox` falls back to the row's own `(qty−pieces)/boxes` — reading it as 0 collapsed a boxed line to loose pieces, and flat-summing repriced a 2-box+5-piece line as 7 BOXES). ⚠️ **`UpdateOrderItemsDto.replaceAll` is now EXPLICIT-ONLY** — the "every item lacks an id ⇒ replace" heuristic is GONE (it is exactly the mobile per-scan shape and wiped orders), so `foldMergeItems`' absolute totals pass `replaceAll: true` out loud. `updateOrderItems` pre-scans every new catalog-linked add inside the transaction and aborts the whole request on an unresolvable id (the old `if (!product) continue` returned HTTP 200 with a dropped line). NEW `GET /buyer/products/scan/:code` — customer-role scanning had NO reachable rung (both product endpoints are `@Roles(OPERATOR,DRIVER)`); it reuses `normalizeScanCode`/`pickBestScanMatch` against the injected prisma and routes the hit through `catalogService.getProductDetail` so the existing `isActive` + regulated visibility gate still applies. Its **client leg ships here too** — mobile `lib/api/buyer.ts#resolveBuyerProductByCode(code, signal?)` (404 = the MISS outcome, deliberately indistinguishable between "no candidate matched" and "hidden from this buyer"; anything else propagates so the buyer is told "try again", never a false "not found") behind NEW `app/(customer)/scan.tsx` (`BarcodeScanner continuous`, entered from a `barcode-outline` NavBar button on `(customer)/(tabs)/catalog.tsx`), the FIRST customer-side scan affordance; a hit adds through the same `cartStore.add` the catalog tile's + button uses, and the lookup is wrapped in `SCAN_RESOLVE_TIMEOUT_MS`'s `AbortController` for the same reason the operator ladder is. ⚠️ A pushed sibling Stack screen, not an in-tab `BarcodeFab` overlay — the tab bar is rendered outside the tab screen, so an absolute-fill overlay inside `(tabs)` leaves the camera under it. ⚠️ Without that caller the route is dead code and R12 is still 403-by-absence — `lib/barcode-resolve.ts` cannot be reused from the buyer realm (both its rungs are staff-gated), which is why this is a separate function on `buyerApiClient`. Web (mirror-sync, R5 only): `apps/web/lib/barcode-resolve.ts` got the SAME `archived` variant + `archivedMessage()`, dropped the search rung's `isActive: true` (limit 10→20) and a sellable-only `matches` list, and its four committing callers (`inventory/page.tsx` restock, `orders/[id]` `EditableLineItems`, `CreateOrderModal`, `components/inventory/StockCountTab.tsx`) now branch on `archived` instead of adding it silently or offering "create product" for a code the catalog already has — without this the golden-reference surface disagreed with mobile about the exact rung R5 names. Specs: `orders.scan-hardening.spec.ts` + seven new mobile suites. Pipeline artifacts: `.claude/pipeline/2026-08-31-f30-scan-loss/`.
- **2026-08-31** — (branch `fix/F04-pricing-mirrors`, off master 77a8c058 → merges post-5e7aba1b; no schema change; MONEY KERNEL) — **F04: the pricing mirrors corrected (B50/B109/B122).** roundMoney = decimal-string half-away-from-zero in ALL FIVE in-repo copies (three mirrors + repair-integrity.mjs + feature-smoke.mjs — the scripts write live ledgers); roundUnitCost (web+mobile 4dp) same treatment with FP-victim pins over both copies. applyBestPromotion selects by the BILLED subtotal on the full denomination ($181.05 not $235.00; orders.service call sites hand the kernel boxes/pieces — the kernel-only fix was inert, spec R7b). prorateLineSubtotal = the paid-basis telescope in all three mirrors with a 5th freeUnitSize param AND the basisQty CAP (Fable final-pass major #2: uncapped, a mixed BOGO partial billed up to 150% of the agreed line; over-delivery now also caps — deliberate contract change, the guard pin fired as designed). G3 parity spec: one hand-worked fixture table over api/web/mobile incl. the tier ladder. ⚠️ Handed to F03's lane: verify the server oracle (buildInvoiceItemData) for the same uncapped region. OWED (D4): a read-only damage report for historically over-billed mixed BOGO lines — customer-credit territory, owner-gated (board #517 note).
- **2026-08-31** — (branch `fix/F04-pricing-mirrors`, off master `77a8c058`; no schema change) — **F04 MAP PASS: THE PRICING TRIPLE MIRROR NOW DOCUMENTS THE PAID-BASIS TELESCOPE, NOT THE OLD LINEAR RATIO.** `mobile.md`'s POS-7 short-pick row still described `prorateLineSubtotal` as "VERBATIM server `orders.service.ts:3321` = `roundMoney(storedSubtotal*deliveredQty/orderQty)`" — false on all three counts after this batch, and the single most dangerous kind of stale map entry (a future session reading the map instead of the repo would be told the driver's at-door proration is a plain linear ratio and could reintroduce REG-B50). Rewritten, and the two NEW copies that had no entry at all are now mapped: `api.md` `common/pricing.ts` and `web.md` `lib/pricing.ts` both carry `prorateLineSubtotal(storedSubtotal, deliveredQty, orderQty, freeUnits = 0, freeUnitSize = 1)` — money off the **STORED** subtotal, reference oracle **`invoices.service.ts#buildInvoiceItemData`** (not `orders.service.ts`), the paid-basis floored cumulative telescope (prorate over `orderQty − freeUnits × freeUnitSize`, floor the free units through the delivered prefix), reducing to the linear formula only at `freeUnits = 0` so 3-arg callers are unaffected; api is the reference implementation with no server caller, web is mirror-only, mobile's `short-pick.ts#reconciledTotal` is the one production caller. `mobile.md` also gained **`short-pick.ts#freeUnitSizeFor`** — the boxes-vs-pieces axis bridge (`unitsPerBox` when `boxes != null`, SALE-TIME snapshot preferred over the live product, else 1) that BOTH driver screens must map through, plus `__tests__/short-pick-prorate.test.ts`. Parity across the three mirrors is pinned by the new `common/pricing-parity.spec.ts` + `pricing-parity.fixtures.ts` (imports the web and mobile copies by relative path). Also corrected in the same `api.md` entry: `applyBestPromotion`'s selection is now the **bill basis** (each candidate priced through `computeLineSubtotal` on the FULL entered quantity, least-billing wins) rather than the documented `(base − net) × qtyUnits` / `freeUnits × base` whole-selling-unit comparison, and `PromoContext`'s new `boxes`/`pieces`/`unitsPerBox` are **opt-in** — a call site that omits them silently degrades to the old basis (REG-B109). Docs only; no code touched.

- **2026-08-30** — (branch `fix/F00-ci-campaign` = ci/minutes-diet + campaign scaffolding, rebased-by-merge onto master 7213ac29; no schema change) — **F00: THE BUG-REGISTER CAMPAIGN'S MACHINERY SHIPS WITH THE CI DIET.** New tracked surfaces: `.claude/campaign/status/F00–F29.jsonl` (the 180-row proof ledger, one shard per batch — shards, not one file, so three concurrently-authored batches never touch the same file and a duplicate ID is a loud error), `scripts/campaign-check.mjs` (the gate: reconciles every affirmative ledger claim against jest/Playwright JSON run artifacts and the claiming batch's own build-plan manual table; `already-fixed`/`refuted` discharge on evidence alone — fixed here, they previously forced a run artifact nothing would read), `.claude/pipeline/fix-cards/F02–F29` (per-batch briefs so dev-pipeline runs never re-read the 533KB register), `.claude/campaign/DECISIONS.md` (D1: no web unit runner — web logic stays T2, proven post-deploy; D2: B126/B127 stay `already-fixed` on #506's evidence), and `.claude/campaign/citation-reanchor-log.md` (1,176 citations audited against 6c8f1401; 13 corrected; 47 multi-candidate flags routed to per-batch discovery). Wiring: `npm run verify` step 3 is now `campaign-check` (before turbo — every PR and the pre-push hook enforce the ledger); `turbo.json` `test.inputs` → `["$TURBO_DEFAULT$"]` (⚠️ the old hand-picked list had NO mobile code dirs — apps/mobile has no `src/`, so mobile test edits busted no hash and turbo replayed cached passes with green jest summaries for code it never ran); `apps/web/playwright.config.ts` gains a `json` reporter → `.campaign/runs/web-e2e.json` (gitignored with the whole `.campaign/` dir) for the gate's T2/post-deploy reads. ⚠️ Merging this PR is ALSO the live test of the `deployment_status` E2E trigger below — watch for an "E2E (Playwright)" run within ~20 min of the first master deploy, else apply ci.yml's two-line revert.
- **2026-08-30** — (branch `ci/minutes-diet`, off master 6c8f1401; no schema change; follow-on to the CI cut below) — **E2E NOW STARTS FROM RAILWAY'S DEPLOY SIGNAL, NOT THE MASTER PUSH — THE ~320s OF PAID SLEEP IS GONE.** This was option 1 of the four priced in ci.yml's e2e header, and the only one that gives up nothing. ⚠️ **THE DESIGN'S ASSUMED MECHANISM WAS UNNECESSARY.** It called for wiring a Railway webhook to `repository_dispatch: [railway-deploy]`, which cannot work directly: GitHub's `/dispatches` endpoint authenticates by `Authorization` header and a Railway webhook cannot set one, so that route needs a PAT **and** a relay service. It is not needed — **Railway's GitHub App is already posting GitHub Deployments to this repo**: `creator: railway-app[bot]`, `environment: "routeflow / production"`, `sha` = the deployed commit, with `in_progress` → `success` statuses, verified present on every master commit back through 2026-08-28 **while the repo was private**. So `on: deployment_status` gets the identical signal with **zero secrets, zero external infrastructure and zero Railway dashboard changes**. `repository_dispatch: [railway-deploy]` stays DECLARED on ci.yml as the fallback, and was REMOVED from `post-deploy.yml` so exactly one workflow consumes it (two consumers = two Playwright suites, ~11 duplicate billed min, the first time anyone wired it). `post-deploy.yml` is now manual-only. ⚠️ **THE FRESHNESS GUARD IS NOT OPTIONAL AND MUST NOT BE "SIMPLIFIED" AWAY.** Railway posts MORE `success` events than there are deploys — measured 18 successes across the 12 most recent deployments — because when deployment N goes live Railway **re-posts `success` onto deployment N-1** (and sometimes N-2), ~4s later (19:49:41 then 19:49:45). Those carry the OLD commit as `deployment.sha`. Acting on one checks out a superseded commit, finds production correctly serving a newer one, and burns the readiness gate's full 15 min before failing: a false red AND ~17 billed min. The guard is the job's FIRST step, before the checkout, `gh api repos/$GITHUB_REPOSITORY/commits/<default_branch>`; not-the-tip ⇒ `run=false` and every later step is `if:`-gated off. It never fails the job, and on an API blip it proceeds rather than skipping — a lost E2E is worse than a wasted one. ⚠️ For the same reason the e2e job's `concurrency` group is **per-SHA, deliberately not a constant**: a constant group with `cancel-in-progress` would let the stale event (arriving 4s later) CANCEL the run for the commit that actually shipped, and then skip itself — E2E would silently never run. ⚠️ `verify`'s `if:` was rewritten from `github.event_name != 'push'` to an explicit allow-list: the negative form would have run the whole correctness suite on every production deploy the moment this workflow gained a third trigger. **Same trap in the e2e gate:** `REQUIRE_SHA` was `github.ref == 'refs/heads/master'`, but on `deployment_status` `github.ref` is empty or a bare SHA, so it would have silently downgraded every deploy-triggered run to the reachability-only path while still reporting green. Both are now event-name allow-lists. `EXPECTED_SHA` and the checkout `ref:` are both `github.event.deployment.sha || github.sha` — they must stay identical or the gate's `git diff` compares the wrong trees. ⚠️ **`ci.yml` HAS NO `push:` TRIGGER ANY MORE.** Re-adding it does not make CI safer, it adds a second full-price E2E per merge; the two-line revert is spelled out in the `on:` block. **This change cannot be tested on a branch** — `deployment_status` and `repository_dispatch` only ever run the DEFAULT BRANCH's copy of a workflow, so the proof is the first master deploy after merge: if no `E2E (Playwright)` run appears within ~20 min of it, revert. PROJECTED (August volume, on top of the CI cut below): e2e 13 → **8 billed min/run**, plus ~41 min/mo of 1-minute stale-event bailouts → **~1,857 optimistic / ~2,076 central / ~2,246 conservative**, vs 2,226-2,705 before. The design quoted 1,816 because it did not know about the duplicate `success` events. Two effects it also did not price, both favourable: a FAILED Railway deploy no longer burns a 15-min gate timeout (~17 billed min) because no `success` is posted, and a SKIPPED deploy costs nothing at all.

- **2026-08-30** — (branch `ci/minutes-diet`, off master 6c8f1401; no schema change) — **CI CUT FROM 6 JOBS TO 1 SO THE REPO CAN STOP FLIPPING PUBLIC.** The repo was going public for a few minutes per merge purely to make Actions minutes free (public = unlimited; private = 2,000/mo on Free). MEASURED FIRST, from the Actions API over 2026-07-31..08-30 (417 runs, per-job wall clock, each job billed rounded UP to the whole minute — reconciles to within 2.3% of the billing API's 6,256 min for August): Test 1,603 · E2E 736 · Type Check 1,172 · Lint 1,152 · Security Audit 949 = **5,612 min/mo = 2.8x the Free allowance.** ⚠️ The `/timing` endpoint returns billable=0 for every one of those runs — that is the public-repo discount, NOT zero usage; compute from job `started_at`/`completed_at` instead. THE BIGGEST LINE ITEM WAS NOT A CHECK: `npm ci` measured 75s inside EACH of five jobs, five times per run, plus five job setups each rounding up to a full billed minute. So lock-integrity + bug-scan + lint + type-check + test + security-audit are now ONE `verify` job, one install, running the literal `npm run verify`. ⚠️ The lockfile validator keeps its PRE-install placement as a STEP (an incoherent override kills `npm ci` itself, so a post-install gate never runs on the case it exists to explain) — the job boundary was never the point, the ordering was. **`verify` no longer runs on the master push** (only PR + dispatch): master is a squash of a rebased, already-verified tree, and Railway's Dockerfiles run `nest build`/`next build` for real, so a type error fails the DEPLOY. Dropped `--coverage` (no `coverageThreshold` is configured anywhere — it gated nothing) and its artifact upload. Dropped the Postgres+Redis services from the test path: **not one of the 181 api specs constructs a PrismaClient or reads DATABASE_URL**, they all mock at the module boundary, so 23s of "Initialize containers" per run served a single 4s check — that check moved to NEW `db-migrations.yml`, `paths:`-filtered to `apps/api/prisma/**`. `prisma db push` deleted outright (it set up a database no test read). `nightly.yml`'s cron COMMENTED OUT — dead since 2026-08-20 (0-step billing reds) and a ~330 min/mo landmine the moment billing is healthy. **LOCAL GATE: `npm run verify` no longer nests `npm run`.** ⚠️ MEASURE GATE TIMINGS ON AN IDLE BOX — the first pass here was taken under load and overstated everything by ~3x. Clean numbers, 3 interleaved rounds: warm gate **39.4s → 30.0s median** (~24%). The mechanism: `npm run` with NO ARGS (it only lists scripts) costs **7-9s** of npm startup on this Windows box vs 0.7s for `node -e ""`, and the old chain paid that three times (outer + 2 nested); the new one pays it once. Under saturation the same npm startup measured ~27s and the old chain 94.5s — so the win scales with how busy the box is, which is when it matters. ⚠️ Editing root `package.json` busts ALL 28 task caches (globalDependencies working as designed) — the very next run is a full cold build, measured 15m14s. ⚠️ That cold run ALSO reproduced the host-saturation false RED: `@routeflow/api#test` died with a bare `exited (1)` and NO jest report; re-run direct on a quiet box gave **181 suites / 3,148 tests, all passing**. Believe the direct re-run, never the bare exit code. `.husky/pre-push` gains a verified-tree fast path keyed on `HEAD^{tree}`, recorded in `.git/rf-verified` ONLY when the working tree is clean (verify inspects files on DISK, so with uncommitted edits a pass does not describe the pushed commit). It is local-only and CI does NOT trust it — a Windows pass is not proof for ubuntu (case-sensitive paths, separators, platform-gated deps), which is exactly what CI's one Linux job is for. ⚠️ Branch protection is unavailable on Free+private (403), so CI is NOT a required check and renaming jobs broke no gate. RESIDUAL: at August's volume the projection is ~2.2-2.5k min/mo, still ABOVE the 2,000 allowance — E2E on every master push (~13 billed min × ~82-102 pushes) is the remaining lever, and `playwright.config.ts`'s `workers: CI ? 1 : 2` is the biggest untaken win.

Scratch file was never committed.

- **2026-08-30** — (branch `fix/destructive-endpoint-guards`, off master `6c8f1401`, no schema
  change) — HARDENED TWO DESTRUCTIVE ENDPOINTS. B126: `DELETE /settings/financial-data`
  (`settings.controller.ts`) was an unscoped `deleteMany({})` across the finance models, wiping
  EVERY tenant's finances from one call and with no confirmation — irreversible and cross-tenant.
  B127: `customers.service.ts deleteAllCustomers` had no PAID/SENT invoice guard, unlike its
  sibling `batchDelete`, so it would destroy paid invoices its neighbor refuses to touch. Fixed:
  B126 now refuses a null tenant (`ForbiddenException`), requires a typed
  `ClearFinancialDataDto.confirmTenantId === caller's own tenantId` (`BadRequestException`
  otherwise), and scopes every delete explicitly — parents on `where: { tenantId }`, children on a
  RELATION filter over the parent's tenantId (`{ invoice: { tenantId } }`, `{ vendorBill: … }`,
  `{ po: … }`, `{ creditNote: … }`), because finance child rows are nullable-tenant and
  nested-created children carry `tenantId: null`. Missing them is fatal, not untidy:
  `InvoicePayment.invoiceId`, `BillPayment.vendorBillId` and `OrderCreditNote.creditNoteId` are ON
  DELETE RESTRICT, so one surviving NULL-tenant child aborts its parent's delete (P2003) and rolls
  the whole wipe back — `orderCreditNote` was added to the cleared set for exactly that reason.
  B127 now runs the same PAID/SENT pre-flight as `batchDelete` before any deletion, and —
  since `DELETE /customers/all` is reachable by SUPER_ADMIN — refuses a null tenant the same
  way B126 does, so the listing, the pre-flight and the wipe can never span every tenant; its
  blocker breakdown is capped at 10 + "and N more" because, unlike `batchDelete`'s, the list
  is every customer in the tenant.
  ⚠️ **Durable lesson — `tenantTransaction` misleads in BOTH directions.** With a null tenant it
  hands back the raw client (`prisma.service.ts` `if (!tenantId) return fn(rawTx)`) — a
  pass-through, not a guard, so a destructive bulk handler must refuse a null tenant itself. With a
  tenant it hands back `_wrapTxWithTenant`, which lists `deleteMany` in `SCOPED_METHODS` and
  rewrites EVERY call as `where: { ...args.where, tenantId }` — silently re-scoping a deliberate
  parent-scoped child filter onto the child's own (null) `tenantId`, i.e. re-creating the P2003
  abort above. So this wipe runs on `prisma.$transaction`, not `tenantTransaction`, and the
  explicit filters plus the null-tenant refusal carry the scoping. Mocks hide this — both
  `createMockPrisma` and the usual hand-rolled fake implement `tenantTransaction` as
  `(fn) => fn(models)` with no injection, so the B126 spec's fake calls the REAL
  `_wrapTxWithTenant` and a regression back to `tenantTransaction` now fails two tests.
  ⚠️ KNOWN LIMITATION, unchanged by this work: `CommissionAccrual.invoiceId` is also RESTRICT and
  is NOT cleared (deleting it would cascade into commission statements/payouts, out of scope), so a
  tenant holding commission accruals still fails this wipe on that FK. Specs:
  `settings.controller.clear-financial.spec.ts`, `customers.service.delete-all.spec.ts`. Map detail
  in `api.md` under `system-config/` and the customers section.
- **2026-08-30 - (branch `docs/product-capability-model`, off master 6c8f1401; DOCS ONLY, no schema change) - NEW `docs/product/` = the PRODUCT CAPABILITY MODEL: 15 domain files + README covering 697 capabilities (207 P0 / 305 P1 / 185 P2) and 230 gaps. Every capability carries a status (SHIPPED/PARTIAL/MISSING/BROKEN) with a CODE ANCHOR plus concrete test criteria (~2,275 checklist lines tagged Jest/Playwright/manual/CI). Built by a 48-agent workflow: one analyst per domain -> one ADVERSARIAL VERIFIER per domain (119 corrections applied; every SHIPPED claim re-checked against its cited route/file and downgraded where the anchor did not hold) -> two whole-product critics (completeness + strategy). WARNING: labels were read against the WORKING TREE at `f932289a` (branch `ci/minutes-diet`), which differs from master ONLY in ci.yml/husky/package.json - no `apps/**` change - so the findings hold for 6c8f1401; the README states the audited commit. HEADLINE FINDING: the per-tenant variation machinery (4 plan keys + 16 FLAG_KEYS + 10 ADDON_SKUS + 5 METER_KEYS + a FIXED-COLUMN `TenantConfig` needing a Prisma migration per toggle + a thin `SystemConfig` k/v store with ~15 keys actually read) is ~70 knobs against 697 capabilities, and EVERY knob answers "does this tenant SEE this module" while NONE answers "how does this capability BEHAVE for this tenant" - so behavioural asks (driver edit replaces vs amends, restock at sale-time vs average cost, credit approval threshold, tax on shipping, business-day boundary) are inexpressible and resolve as per-tenant forks. README argues for a typed/versioned/defaulted POLICY layer beside the entitlement layer. Also independently CONFIRMED while reviewing: `apps/api/src/bookkeeping/invoice.service.ts:86` writes via a bare `this.prisma.transaction.update()` with no tenant scoping. No code was added this session - the only map change is the new INDEX row.
